  Anthropic SWE Take-Home Assignment

  Context: Build something that showcases judgment, creativity, and execution —
  a tool that is useful, trustworthy, and delightful.

  Time: Target 1–2 hours. Hard limit 8 hours. Scoping well is part of the
  evaluation. Note how long you spent in the write-up.

  Objective: Ship an impressive, self-contained prototype. Pick one theme:

  ┌─────┬─────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  #  │      Theme      │                      Focus                      │
  ├─────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │     │ Exploration &   │ Tool for deep understanding — emergent-dynamics │
  │ 1   │ Understanding   │  sim, technical explainer, or explorer for      │
  │     │                 │ codebases/datasets/documents                    │
  ├─────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │     │ Creative &      │ Real creative leverage — genuine control over   │
  │ 2   │ Generative      │ iteration, variation, refinement (not just      │
  │     │ Tools           │ "generate" buttons)                             │
  ├─────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │     │ Systems &       │ Thoughtful systems engineering — dev tool,      │
  │ 3   │ Reliability     │ distributed primitive handling failure, or      │
  │     │                 │ infra that degrades predictably                 │
  ├─────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 4   │ Evaluation &    │ Real challenge in evals, reward modeling, data  │
  │     │ Data Quality    │ quality, or experiment tracking                 │
  ├─────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │     │ Performance     │ Fastest implementation of a meaningful          │
  │ 5   │ Optimization    │ component, with benchmarks vs. naive/reference  │
  │     │                 │ baselines (judged on measured perf)             │
  └─────┴─────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Critical requirement — self-contained evaluation: A reviewer must be able to
  evaluate it without supplying their own data or domain expertise. Bundle
  compelling examples or provide a "demo mode."

  Three deliverables:
  1. Deployed prototype — usable immediately by the Anthropic team. Browser/API
     is simplest; local needs a clean run path. A single focused feature is
     fine; polish < demonstrating the core idea. Include sample data / demo
     mode.
  2. Code — GitHub repo link; any languages/tech.
  3. Design rationale — both a ~5 min self-recorded video and a short written
     doc covering: why this theme + approach; what's non-obvious about it; key
     design decisions and tradeoffs; how you'd extend it; time spent.

  AI use: Expected. Submit your Claude/AI transcripts (Claude Code transcripts
  tool or claude.ai "Share"). They're evaluating how you direct the AI and
  maintain your own vision — a fully AI-generated project with no judgment will
  not pass.

  Submission (via email): GitHub repo link · hosted prototype link · AI
  transcripts · explanation artifacts (video + written doc).

⏺ I dropped a full requirements doc at
  /Users/vksatish/anthropic-swe-takehome/docs/REQUIREMENTS.md. Here's the shape
  of it:

  What it proposes

  Product: Committed-Spend Onboarding Studio — a browser tool that turns a
  signed committed-spend contract into a verified billing configuration. Three
  stages on one screen:

  1. Contract — pick a bundled sample or paste a term sheet → extract terms
     (LLM, with deterministic fallback)
  2. Deal terms — every field editable, each with a source snippet + confidence
  3. Verify & simulate — validation findings (severity + $ impact), commitment
     burn-down chart, per-period invoice, export bundle

  Core thesis: committed-spend billing onboarding is a verification problem, not
  data entry. Treat the contract like source code — parse it, compile it to a
  canonical rate plan, typecheck it against realistic usage.

  The demo payoff: the "Global Enterprise" sample has a seeded transcription
  error (ramp sums to $29M against a $30M headline). The Verify panel catches it
  as RAMP_SUM_MISMATCH — Δ $1,000,000 (ERROR); you fix year 3, it clears live,
  invoice + chart update. Under 2 minutes, zero setup.

  Key sections

  - §7 Deal-terms schema — the extraction target (commitment, ramp, pricing
    modes, rollover, overage, dedicated capacity, credits, true-up)
  - §8 Canonical rate plan — the compiled, billing-system-facing artifact
  - §9 Validation-rule catalog — ~15 rules with severity and dollar-impact
    estimates
  - §10 Bundled demo data — 3 contracts (one clean, two with seeded issues) + 3
    usage traces (steady / ramping / spike)
  - §11 NFRs — self-contained, static deploy, deterministic money math, pure
    unit-tested engine
  - §12 Architecture — Vite + React + TS, pure engine/ (compile / validate /
    simulate), browser SDK call for extraction
  - §15 Time budget — what's in for 2–3h, what gets cut first
  - §16 Open questions — 5 domain decisions (prepaid vs arrears depth,
    cache-token lines, tier basis, true-up semantics, impact estimates without a
    trace) each with a leaning

  Review it and tell me what to change — especially the open questions in §16
  and the scope line in §15. Once you're happy I'll scaffold the project.

         +tees. This turns lumpy usage into predictable contracted revenu

      19 -The revenue only stays predictable if the customer is **billed 
         -exactly what the contract says**. The moment that accuracy is w

         -F full of negotiated prose and a billing system that needs an u
         -nambiguous, machine-executable rate plan. Today that translatio
         -n is manual, done under deal-close time pressure, by someone re
         -ading a term sheet into a config UI.                           
      19 +The revenue only stays predictable if the customer is **billed

         + per token type, per seat, per tool call).

      21 +The core mismatch:                                             
      22

         +-service discount", or "Claude Code 20% off, Claude API 10% off

         +E`, `CLAUDE-ENTERPRISE-SEAT`, …

      26 +Onboarding is the step that resolves the first into the second.
         + Doing it by hand under deal-close pressure produces silent err
         +ors:                                                           
      27 +

      29  | --- | --- |

         - |

      40  ## 3. Goals / non-goals
      41  
      42  ### Goals
      39 -1. Ingest a committed-spend contract (paste text; LLM extractio
         -n) and produce a **structured, reviewable** set of deal terms w
         -ith per-field provenance and confidence.                       
      40 -2. Compile confirmed terms into a **canonical declarative rate 
         -plan** (JSON) — the artifact a billing system would consume.   
      41 -3. **Verify** the rate plan: run a catalog of validation rules 
         -and surface findings with severity and estimated dollar impact.
      42 -4. **Simulate** the rate plan against bundled usage traces: com
         -mitment burn-down, per-period invoice line items, overage, roll
         -over, true-up, credits.                                        
      43 -5. Let the reviewer edit any term, re-simulate live, watch find
         -ings clear.                                                    
      44 -6. **Export** the final config bundle + a human-readable onboar
         -ding summary ("Activate billing account").                     
      45 -7. Work **with zero setup** — no API key, no network, no backen
         -d — via bundled sample contracts with pre-extracted terms.     
      43 +1. **Extraction layer.** Read a customer's signed PDF agreement
         + and capture the required fields into structured JSON: account 
         +number, contract duration, annual spend commitment, and discoun
         +t terms (cross-service **or** service-specific).               
      44 +2. **Internal product catalog.** Maintain SKUs for all products
         + available via Claude, grouped by service, each with a list pri
         +ce and unit.                                                   
      45 +3. **Discount resolution / tagging.** Take the contract's disco
         +unt terms and resolve them to a **per-SKU discount attribute**,
         + with the precedence rule made explicit and the source of every
         + SKU's discount recorded.                                      
      46 +4. **Verification.** Validate the contract JSON and the resolve
         +d SKU schedule — flag missing fields, unknown services, uncover
         +ed SKUs, out-of-range or conflicting discounts — with severity.
      47 +5. **Output for billing.** Export a **SKU rate schedule** (JSON
         + + CSV): for each SKU — list price, discount %, effective price
         +, discount source, account, effective dates. This is the artifa
         +ct the billing team consumes when metering usage.              
      48 +6. **Self-contained.** Full evaluation with no API key, no netw
         +ork, no backend: bundled sample PDFs ship with pre-extracted JS
         +ON; the catalog and resolver run entirely in the browser.      
      49  
      50  ### Non-goals
      48 -- A production billing engine or invoice-of-record. This is a p
         -re-flight tool.                                                
      49 -- Real provisioning / writes to any downstream system ("Activat
         -e" produces a bundle, nothing more).                           
      50 -- OCR / scanned-PDF handling. Input is pasted text or a bundled
         - sample.                                                       
      51 +- A production billing engine, invoice-of-record, or usage mete
         +ring. This is a pre-flight onboarding tool; it produces the rat
         +e schedule, it does not run billing.                           
      52 +- OCR of scanned/handwritten PDFs. Input is a text-based PDF (b

      53 +- Ramp schedules, rollover, true-up, overage mechanics, tiered

         +ment + a discount structure. Tiered/ramped contracts are a futu
         +re extension — §12.)                                           
      54  - FX / multi-currency conversion. Currency is a tag; mixing cur
          rencies is a validation error.
      52 -- Payment terms, tax, SLAs, security schedules — captured only 
         -where they affect the invoice math (mostly not).

       86 -### 6.1 Contract ingestion

          -one loads its term-sheet text and its pre-extracted terms (no

          -ext into the terms schema (§7). Uses `@anthropic-ai/sdk` from 
          -the browser with structured output (Zod schema). Requires a us
          -er-supplied Anthropic API key (stored in `localStorage`, sent 
          -only to `api.anthropic.com`).                                 
       90 -- **FR-4** If no key is set, "Extract terms" is disabled with 
          -a clear message; bundled samples still work fully. The tool is
          - never blocked on the LLM.                                    
       91 -- **FR-5** Extraction returns, per field: value, a verbatim `s
          -ourceSnippet` from the contract, and `confidence` (`high` / `m
          -edium` / `low`).                                              
      102 +### 6.1 Extraction layer                                      
      103 +- **FR-1** Sample picker with ≥3 bundled signed-agreement PDFs
          +; selecting one loads the PDF preview and its **pre-extracted 
          +JSON** (no network).                                          
      104 +- **FR-2** Upload a PDF file (drag-drop or file input).       
      105 +- **FR-3** "Extract" sends the PDF to Claude (browser `@anthro
          +pic-ai/sdk`, PDF as a base64 `document` block, structured outp
          +ut via a Zod schema) and fills the contract JSON.             
      106 +- **FR-4** Extraction requires a user-supplied Anthropic API k
          +ey (stored in `localStorage`, sent only to `api.anthropic.com`
          +). With no key, "Extract" is disabled with a clear message; **
          +bundled samples still work fully**. The tool is never blocked 
          +on the LLM.                                                   
      107 +- **FR-5** Required fields to capture (see schema §7): `accoun
          +tNumber`, `term` (start, end, or duration in years), `annualCo
          +mmitmentUSD`, `currency`, and a `discount` block that is **eit
          +her** cross-service **or** service-specific (optionally both: 
          +a cross-service baseline plus per-service overrides, plus opti
          +onal SKU-level exhibit overrides).                            
      108 +- **FR-6** Each extracted field carries a verbatim `sourceSnip
          +pet` from the PDF and a `confidence` (`high` / `medium` / `low
          +`).                                                           
      109  
       93 -### 6.2 Term review (human-in-the-loop)

          -nd unset-but-required fields are visually flagged.            
       95 -- **FR-7** Each field shows its source snippet on hover/expand
          - so the reviewer can check the AI against the contract.       
       96 -- **FR-8** Editing a term immediately recompiles the rate plan
          - and re-runs verification + simulation.                       
      110 +### 6.2 Contract review (human-in-the-loop)                   
      111 +- **FR-7** Every extracted field is editable. Low-confidence a
          +nd missing-required fields are visually flagged.              
      112 +- **FR-8** Each field can reveal its source snippet so the rev
          +iewer can check the AI against the document.                  
      113 +- **FR-9** Any edit immediately re-resolves the SKU schedule a
          +nd re-runs verification.                                      
      114  
       98 -### 6.3 Compile to rate plan                                  
       99 -- **FR-9** Confirmed terms compile deterministically into the 
          -canonical rate plan JSON (§8). The compiled JSON is viewable a
          -nd copyable.                                                  
      100 -- **FR-10** Compilation is pure and total: invalid/incomplete 
          -terms produce a rate plan plus findings, never a crash.       
      115 +### 6.3 Product catalog                                       
      116 +- **FR-10** A bundled catalog of **services**, each containing
          + **SKUs**. SKU: `id`, `service`, `displayName`, `unit` (`per_m
          +tok` | `per_seat_month` | `per_1k_calls`), `listPrice`, `meter
          +Key`.                                                         
      117 +- **FR-11** Services in the demo catalog: **Claude API** (per 
          +model × token line — input, output, cache-write-5m, cache-writ
          +e-1h, cache-read; for Opus 5 / Sonnet 5 / Haiku 4.5, plus Batc
          +h variants), **Claude Code**, **Claude for Work** (Team / Ente
          +rprise seats), **Server Tools** (web search, code execution). 
          +~24 SKUs total.                                               
      118 +- **FR-12** List prices come from a dated `priceBook` constant
          +; prices not publicly published are clearly labelled *illustra
          +tive*.                                                        
      119 +- **FR-13** Catalog is read-only in the UI for this build (edi
          +ting SKUs is a future extension), but viewable as a reference 
          +panel/drawer.                                                 
      120  
      102 -### 6.4 Verify                                                
      103 -- **FR-11** Run the validation-rule catalog (§9) over terms + 
          -compiled plan.                                                
      104 -- **FR-12** Each finding: `code`, `severity` (`ERROR` / `WARNI
          -NG` / `INFO`), human message, affected field(s), and `estimate
          -dImpactUSD` where computable.                                 
      105 -- **FR-13** Findings sorted ERROR → WARNING → INFO. Export is 
          -allowed with warnings, discouraged with errors (soft gate — re
          -viewer can override).                                         
      121 +### 6.4 Discount resolution / tagging                         
      122 +- **FR-14** Resolve a `discountPct` and a `source` for **every
          + SKU in the catalog**, using this precedence (highest wins):  
      123 +  1. `sku_override` — a discount named for that exact SKU in a
          + contract exhibit                                             
      124 +  2. `service:<name>` — a service-specific discount whose serv
          +ice matches the SKU's service                                 
      125 +  3. `cross_service` — the contract's cross-service baseline d
          +iscount                                                       
      126 +  4. `none` — no rule reaches this SKU → `discountPct = 0`    
      127 +- **FR-15** Produce the **SKU rate schedule**: for each SKU — 
          +`skuId`, `service`, `unit`, `listPrice`, `discountPct`, `effec
          +tivePrice` (= list × (1 − pct/100), rounded per §11), `source`
          +, plus contract `accountNumber` and `effectiveFrom` / `effecti
          +veTo`.                                                        
      128 +- **FR-16** Show a resolution summary: count of SKUs by source
          +, weighted-average discount (weighted by list price as a rough
          + proxy), and the list of SKUs resolving to `none`.            
      129  
      107 -### 6.5 Simulate                                              
      108 -- **FR-14** Bundled usage traces (≥3: steady, ramping, spiky).
          - User picks one.                                              
      109 -- **FR-15** The billing engine runs the trace through the comp
          -iled plan and produces, per billing period: usage charges by m
          -odel & token type, discounts applied, dedicated-capacity fees,
          - commitment drawdown, remaining balance, overage, rollover in/
          -out, true-up, credit consumption, invoice total.              
      110 -- **FR-16** Commitment **burn-down chart** across the full ter
          -m (committed line vs. consumed line vs. balance).             
      111 -- **FR-17** Expandable **invoice** for a selected period with 
          -itemized lines.                                               
      112 -- **FR-18** Simulation-derived findings: projected forfeited/b
          -reakage $, periods with overage, run-rate vs. commitment gap. 
      113 -- **FR-19** Re-simulation on any edit completes in < 100 ms (f
          -eels live).                                                   
      130 +### 6.5 Verification                                          
      131 +- **FR-17** Run the validation-rule catalog (§9) over the cont
          +ract JSON + resolved schedule.                                
      132 +- **FR-18** Each finding: `code`, `severity` (`ERROR` / `WARNI
          +NG` / `INFO`), message, affected field(s) or SKU(s).          
      133 +- **FR-19** Findings sorted ERROR → WARNING → INFO. Export all
          +owed with warnings, soft-gated with errors (reviewer can overr
          +ide).                                                         
      134  
      115 -### 6.6 Export / activate                                     
      116 -- **FR-20** "Export config bundle" downloads a single JSON: ca
          -nonical rate plan + terms + provenance + validation report + t
          -ool version + timestamp.                                      
      117 -- **FR-21** "Onboarding summary" renders a human-readable reca
          -p (commitment, term, ramp, pricing, key policies, open warning
          -s).                                                           
      135 +### 6.6 Export                                                
      136 +- **FR-20** "Export JSON" — one file: contract JSON + resolved
          + SKU rate schedule + provenance + validation report + tool ver
          +sion + timestamp.                                             
      137 +- **FR-21** "Export CSV" — the SKU rate schedule as a flat tab
          +le (`sku_id, service, unit, list_price, discount_pct, effectiv
          +e_price, discount_source, account, effective_from, effective_t
          +o`) for direct load into a billing system.

      211  ## 9. Validation-rule catalog (initial)
      212  
      196 -| Code | Severity | Fires when | Impact estimate |            
      197 -| --- | --- | --- | --- |                                     
      198 -| `RAMP_SUM_MISMATCH` | ERROR | Σ ramp amounts ≠ commitment.to
          -talUSD | Δ = \|Σ − total\| |                                  
      199 -| `TERM_DATES_INVALID` | ERROR | endDate ≤ startDate, or dates
          - missing | — |                                                
      200 -| `RAMP_PERIOD_GAP_OR_OVERLAP` | ERROR | Ramp periods not cont
          -iguous / overlap / don't span the term | — |                  
      201 -| `TIERS_OVERLAP_OR_GAP` | ERROR | For a model, tier ranges ov
          -erlap or leave a hole below the max observed usage | est. from
          - trace |                                                      
      202 -| `DISCOUNT_OUT_OF_RANGE` | ERROR | Any discountPct < 0 or > 1
          -00 | — |                                                      
      203 -| `CURRENCY_MIX` | ERROR | Commitment / credits / capacity fee
          -s not all in `currency` | — |                                 
      204 -| `BILLING_PERIOD_INDIVISIBLE` | ERROR | Term length not a who
          -le number of billing periods | — |                            
      205 -| `OVERAGE_POLICY_UNSET` | WARNING | Overage possible but poli
          -cy missing → defaults to list | trace overage × list |        
      206 -| `CAPACITY_EXCEEDS_COMMITMENT` | WARNING | Σ dedicated-capaci
          -ty fees in a period > that period's commitment | fee − commitm
          -ent |                                                         
      207 -| `ROLLOVER_UNSPECIFIED` | WARNING | No rollover policy → assu
          -med forfeit | projected breakage |                            
      208 -| `TRUEUP_CADENCE_LOOSER_THAN_BILLING` | WARNING | True-up les
          -s frequent than billing frequency | — |                       
      209 -| `RAMP_DECREASING` | WARNING | A ramp period is smaller than 
          -the previous | — |                                            
      210 -| `CREDIT_EXPIRES_BEFORE_USABLE` | WARNING | Credit expiry pre
          -cedes the date the ramp lets it be consumed | credit amount | 
      211 -| `RUN_RATE_INFEASIBLE` | INFO | Implied $/mo run-rate ≫ or ≪ 
          -selected trace's consumption | projected shortfall/overage |  
      212 -| `PROJECTED_BREAKAGE` | INFO | Simulation forfeits commitment
          - at a period boundary | forfeited $ |                         
      213 +| Code | Severity | Fires when |                              
      214 +| --- | --- | --- |                                           
      215 +| `ACCOUNT_NUMBER_MISSING` | ERROR | `accountNumber` empty |  
      216 +| `ACCOUNT_NUMBER_MALFORMED` | ERROR | doesn't match expected 
          +pattern `^[A-Z]{3}-\d{6}$` |                                  
      217 +| `TERM_UNRESOLVABLE` | ERROR | can't derive both start and en
          +d (no dates and no duration) |                                
      218 +| `TERM_DATES_INVALID` | ERROR | `endDate` ≤ `startDate` |    
      219 +| `COMMITMENT_NOT_POSITIVE` | ERROR | `annualCommitmentUSD` ≤ 
          +0 or missing |                                                
      220 +| `NO_DISCOUNT_TERMS` | ERROR | none of cross-service / servic
          +e / SKU discounts present |                                   
      221 +| `DISCOUNT_PCT_OUT_OF_RANGE` | ERROR | any pct < 0 or > 100 |
      222 +| `UNKNOWN_SERVICE_IN_CONTRACT` | ERROR | a `serviceOverrides[
          +].service` has no match in the catalog |                      
      223 +| `UNKNOWN_SKU_IN_EXHIBIT` | ERROR | a `skuOverrides[].skuId` 
          +is not in the catalog |                                       
      224 +| `CURRENCY_MIX` | ERROR | contract currency differs from a ca
          +talog price's currency (all demo prices USD) |                
      225 +| `SKU_UNCOVERED` | WARNING | a catalog SKU resolves to `sourc
          +e = none` (list them) |                                       
      226 +| `SERVICE_PARTIALLY_COVERED` | WARNING | some SKUs of a servi
          +ce take an override and others fall through to a *lower* disco
          +unt |                                                         
      227 +| `OVERRIDE_WORSE_THAN_BASELINE` | WARNING | a service/SKU ove
          +rride pct < `crossServicePct` (customer gets a worse rate on a
          + named line — confirm intent) |                               
      228 +| `LOW_CONFIDENCE_FIELD` | WARNING | extraction confidence `lo
          +w` on a required field |                                      
      229 +| `WEIGHTED_AVG_DISCOUNT` | INFO | reports the list-price-weig
          +hted average discount across the catalog |

          + implied monthly run-rate, for sanity |

      253  ## 11. Non-functional requirements
      254  
      234 -- **NFR-1 Self-contained.** Full evaluation with no API key, n
          -o network, no backend. Demo mode = bundled contracts + pre-ext
          -racted terms + bundled traces.                                
      255 +- **NFR-1 Self-contained.** Full evaluation with no API key, n
          +o network, no backend. Demo mode = bundled PDFs + pre-extracte
          +d JSON + bundled catalog.                                     
      256  - **NFR-2 Deployable as static assets.** `vite build` → any st
           atic host (GitHub Pages / Vercel / Netlify). No server, no env
            vars required to run.
      236 -- **NFR-3 Deterministic money math.** Integer minor units (or 
          -`decimal.js`); no floating-point drift; every line reconciles 
          -to the invoice total exactly.                                 
      237 -- **NFR-4 Live feel.** Initial load < 2 s on a cold cache; ful
          -l recompile + re-verify + re-simulate < 100 ms.               
      238 -- **NFR-5 Engine is pure and unit-tested.** `compile()`, `vali
          -date()`, `simulate()` are framework-free pure functions with V
          -itest coverage on the rule catalog and at least one full trace
          --through-invoice reconciliation per sample contract.          
      239 -- **NFR-6 Secret hygiene.** API key only in `localStorage`; on
          -ly ever sent to `api.anthropic.com`; never logged; never in th
          -e export bundle.                                              
      240 -- **NFR-7 Legible under demo.** Keyboard-navigable forms, visi
          -ble focus, findings readable at a glance.                     
      257 +- **NFR-3 Deterministic money math.** Prices held as decimal s
          +trings or integer minor units; `effectivePrice` rounding rule 
          +stated once (round half-up to 6 significant places / to the ce
          +nt for seats) and applied everywhere; CSV and JSON agree exact
          +ly.                                                           
      258 +- **NFR-4 Live feel.** Initial load < 2 s cold; full re-resolv
          +e + re-verify < 50 ms.                                        
      259 +- **NFR-5 Pure, unit-tested engine.** `resolve()` and `validat
          +e()` are framework-free pure functions with Vitest coverage: t
          +he rule catalog, the precedence table, and a full expected-sch
          +edule snapshot per sample contract.                           
      260 +- **NFR-6 Secret hygiene.** API key only in `localStorage`; on
          +ly ever sent to `api.anthropic.com`; never logged; never in an
          + export.                                                      
      261 +- **NFR-7 Legible under demo.** Keyboard-navigable forms, visi
          +ble focus, findings readable at a glance, SKU table sortable/f
          +ilterable by service and source.

     ...

      268  src/
      269    engine/
      249 -    priceBook.ts       # dated Anthropic list prices for demo 
          -models                                                        
      250 -    terms.ts           # DealTerms zod schema + types         
      251 -    compile.ts         # DealTerms -> RatePlan   (pure)

          -fer. *Leaning: make it a term (`trueUp.shortfallBilled: boolea

          +Us; a contract that wants to exclude a service says so with a 
          +0% override.*

⏺ Rewrote docs/REQUIREMENTS.md around your framing. Here's what changed and what
  it now says:

  The pivot

  ┌─────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Was         │                        Now                        │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Paste term-sheet    │ Signed PDF in; extraction layer → contract JSON   │
  │ text                │                                                   │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Ramp / rollover /   │ Dropped. Contract shape = flat annual commitment  │
  │ true-up / tiers /   │ + a discount structure                            │
  │ overage             │                                                   │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Usage simulation +  │ Deferred to §12 future. Output is the rate        │
  │ invoice preview     │ schedule, not a simulated bill                    │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                     │ Internal product catalog — SKUs grouped by        │
  │ —                   │ service, each with list price + unit (~24 SKUs    │
  │                     │ across Claude API, Claude Code, Claude for Work,  │
  │                     │ Server Tools)                                     │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                     │ Discount resolution / tagging — resolve every     │
  │ —                   │ catalog SKU to a discountPct + source via a       │
  │                     │ precedence rule                                   │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ —                   │ SKU rate schedule export (JSON + CSV) — the       │
  │                     │ artifact the billing team meters against          │
  └─────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Discount model

  Contract discount block captures either or both:
  - crossServicePct — baseline across all SKUs
  - serviceOverrides[] — e.g. Claude Code 20%, Claude API 10%
  - skuOverrides[] — rare, from an appendix/exhibit (e.g. API-OPUS-5-OUTPUT 30%)

  Precedence (highest wins): sku_override → service:<name> → cross_service →
  none (0%). Every SKU's row records which one it got.

  Demo data (self-contained)

  3 generated sample PDFs + pre-extracted JSON:
  - Northwind — cross-service 12%, clean happy path
  - Meridian — service-specific only; Server Tools unnamed → SKU_UNCOVERED
    warnings
  - Atlas — malformed account number (ERROR) + names a nonexistent service
    (ERROR) + exercises all 3 precedence levels

  Decisions I need from you (§15)

  1. Does "cross-service" cover Server Tools and seats, or only consumption (API
     + Claude Code)? — I leaned "all SKUs".
  2. Account number pattern — I assumed AAA-NNNNNN. Real one?
  3. Batch SKUs — contract discount stacks on the already-50%-off Batch price,
     or applies to standard then Batch? — I leaned "stacks on the Batch list
     price".
  4. Effective price — emit discount % only (let billing compute price), or also
     emit a rounded effective price? — I leaned "both, % is authoritative".
  5. Duplicate service override for the same service — flag as ERROR vs. pick
     max/last? — I leaned ERROR.

  Tell me your calls on those (or "your leanings are fine") and anything else to
  adjust, then I'll scaffold.

      121  ## 6. Functional requirements
      122  
      102 -### 6.1 Extraction layer                                      
      103 -- **FR-1** Sample picker with ≥3 bundled signed-agreement PDFs
          -; selecting one loads the PDF preview and its **pre-extracted 
          -JSON** (no network).                                          
      104 -- **FR-2** Upload a PDF file (drag-drop or file input).       
      105 -- **FR-3** "Extract" sends the PDF to Claude (browser `@anthro
          -pic-ai/sdk`, PDF as a base64 `document` block, structured outp
          -ut via a Zod schema) and fills the contract JSON.             
      106 -- **FR-4** Extraction requires a user-supplied Anthropic API k
          -ey (stored in `localStorage`, sent only to `api.anthropic.com`
          -). With no key, "Extract" is disabled with a clear message; **
          -bundled samples still work fully**. The tool is never blocked 
          -on the LLM.                                                   
      107 -- **FR-5** Required fields to capture (see schema §7): `accoun
          -tNumber`, `term` (start, end, or duration in years), `annualCo
          -mmitmentUSD`, `currency`, and a `discount` block that is **eit
          -her** cross-service **or** service-specific (optionally both: 
          -a cross-service baseline plus per-service overrides, plus opti
          -onal SKU-level exhibit overrides).                            
      108 -- **FR-6** Each extracted field carries a verbatim `sourceSnip
          -pet` from the PDF and a `confidence` (`high` / `medium` / `low
          -`).                                                           
      123 +### 6.1 Product catalog (shared)                              
      124 +- **FR-1** Bundled catalog of **services**, each containing **
          +SKUs**. SKU: `id`, `service`, `displayName`, `unit` (`per_mtok
          +` | `per_seat_month` | `per_1k_calls`), `listPrice`, `meterKey
          +`.                                                            
      125 +- **FR-2** Demo services: **Claude API** (per model × token li
          +ne — input, output, cache-write-5m, cache-write-1h, cache-read
          + — for Opus 5 / Sonnet 5 / Haiku 4.5, plus Batch variants), **
          +Claude Code**, **Claude for Work** (Team / Enterprise seats), 
          +**Server Tools** (web search, code execution). ~24 SKUs.      
      126 +- **FR-3** List prices from a dated `priceBook` constant; non-
          +public prices labelled *illustrative*.                        
      127 +- **FR-4** Catalog is read-only in this build; viewable as a d
          +rawer.                                                        
      128  
      110 -### 6.2 Contract review (human-in-the-loop)                   
      111 -- **FR-7** Every extracted field is editable. Low-confidence a
          -nd missing-required fields are visually flagged.              
      112 -- **FR-8** Each field can reveal its source snippet so the rev
          -iewer can check the AI against the document.                  
      113 -- **FR-9** Any edit immediately re-resolves the SKU schedule a
          -nd re-runs verification.                                      
      129 +### 6.2 Discount resolution engine (shared)                   
      130 +- **FR-5** A discount structure is: optional `crossServicePct`
          +, optional `serviceOverrides[] {service, pct}`, optional `skuO
          +verrides[] {skuId, pct}`.                                     
      131 +- **FR-6** `resolve(discountStructure, catalog)` returns, **fo
          +r every catalog SKU**, a `discountPct` and a `source`, using p
          +recedence (highest wins): `sku_override` → `service:<name>` → 
          +`cross_service` → `none` (0%).                                
      132 +- **FR-7** Output the **SKU rate schedule**: per SKU — `skuId`
          +, `service`, `unit`, `listPrice`, `discountPct`, `effectivePri
          +ce` (= `listPrice × (1 − pct/100)`, rounding per §11), `source
          +`.                                                            
      133 +- **FR-8** Resolution summary: counts by source, list-price-we
          +ighted average discount, and the list of SKUs resolving to `no
          +ne`.                                                          
      134  
      115 -### 6.3 Product catalog                                       
      116 -- **FR-10** A bundled catalog of **services**, each containing
          - **SKUs**. SKU: `id`, `service`, `displayName`, `unit` (`per_m
          -tok` | `per_seat_month` | `per_1k_calls`), `listPrice`, `meter
          -Key`.                                                         
      117 -- **FR-11** Services in the demo catalog: **Claude API** (per 
          -model × token line — input, output, cache-write-5m, cache-writ
          -e-1h, cache-read; for Opus 5 / Sonnet 5 / Haiku 4.5, plus Batc
          -h variants), **Claude Code**, **Claude for Work** (Team / Ente
          -rprise seats), **Server Tools** (web search, code execution). 
          -~24 SKUs total.                                               
      118 -- **FR-12** List prices come from a dated `priceBook` constant
          -; prices not publicly published are clearly labelled *illustra
          -tive*.                                                        
      119 -- **FR-13** Catalog is read-only in the UI for this build (edi
          -ting SKUs is a future extension), but viewable as a reference 
          -panel/drawer.                                                 
      135 +### 6.3 Onboard mode — extraction                             
      136 +- **FR-9** Sample picker: ≥3 bundled signed-agreement PDFs; se
          +lecting one loads a PDF preview + its **pre-extracted JSON** (
          +no network).                                                  
      137 +- **FR-10** Upload a PDF (drag-drop or file input).           
      138 +- **FR-11** "Extract" → Claude (browser `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, P
          +DF as base64 `document` block, structured output via Zod) fill
          +s the contract JSON.                                          
      139 +- **FR-12** Extraction needs a user-supplied Anthropic API key
          + (`localStorage`; sent only to `api.anthropic.com`). No key → 
          +"Extract" disabled with a clear message; **bundled samples sti
          +ll fully work**.                                              
      140 +- **FR-13** Required fields: `accountNumber`, `term` (start + 
          +end, or duration years), `annualCommitmentUSD`, `currency`, an
          +d a `discount` structure (at least one of the three parts popu
          +lated). Each field carries a verbatim `sourceSnippet` and `con
          +fidence` (`high`/`medium`/`low`).                             
      141  
      121 -### 6.4 Discount resolution / tagging                         
      122 -- **FR-14** Resolve a `discountPct` and a `source` for **every
          - SKU in the catalog**, using this precedence (highest wins):  
      123 -  1. `sku_override` — a discount named for that exact SKU in a
          - contract exhibit                                             
      124 -  2. `service:<name>` — a service-specific discount whose serv
          -ice matches the SKU's service                                 
      125 -  3. `cross_service` — the contract's cross-service baseline d
          -iscount                                                       
      126 -  4. `none` — no rule reaches this SKU → `discountPct = 0`    
      127 -- **FR-15** Produce the **SKU rate schedule**: for each SKU — 
          -`skuId`, `service`, `unit`, `listPrice`, `discountPct`, `effec
          -tivePrice` (= list × (1 − pct/100), rounded per §11), `source`
          -, plus contract `accountNumber` and `effectiveFrom` / `effecti
          -veTo`.                                                        
      128 -- **FR-16** Show a resolution summary: count of SKUs by source
          -, weighted-average discount (weighted by list price as a rough
          - proxy), and the list of SKUs resolving to `none`.            
      142 +### 6.4 Onboard mode — review & output                        
      143 +- **FR-14** Every extracted field editable; low-confidence / m
          +issing-required flagged; source snippet revealable.           
      144 +- **FR-15** Any edit re-resolves + re-verifies live.          
      145 +- **FR-16** "Export JSON" — contract JSON + resolved SKU sched
          +ule + provenance + validation report + tool version + timestam
          +p.                                                            
      146 +- **FR-17** "Export CSV" — the SKU rate schedule as a flat tab
          +le (`sku_id, service, unit, list_price, discount_pct, effectiv
          +e_price, discount_source, account, effective_from, effective_t
          +o`).                                                          
      147  
      130 -### 6.5 Verification                                          
      131 -- **FR-17** Run the validation-rule catalog (§9) over the cont
          -ract JSON + resolved schedule.                                
      132 -- **FR-18** Each finding: `code`, `severity` (`ERROR` / `WARNI
          -NG` / `INFO`), message, affected field(s) or SKU(s).          
      133 -- **FR-19** Findings sorted ERROR → WARNING → INFO. Export all
          -owed with warnings, soft-gated with errors (reviewer can overr
          -ide).                                                         
      148 +### 6.5 Model-a-deal mode — inputs                            
      149 +- **FR-18** Bundled **historical usage** dataset per prospect:
          + ≥6 months, each month a list of `{ skuId, quantity, listAmoun
          +tUSD, paidAmountUSD }` (see §8).                              
      150 +- **FR-19** Prospect picker (bundled). (CSV upload of usage is
          + a future extension.)                                         
      151 +- **FR-20** **Commitment input**: a numeric field + slider ove
          +r a sensible range (e.g. $0–$40M/yr).                         
      152 +- **FR-21** **Commitment → discount tier table** (bundled, §9)
          +: the chosen commitment selects a tier → a discount structure.
          + Show which tier is active and its bounds.                    
      153 +- **FR-22** Optional **manual discount override**: the user ca
          +n hand-edit the discount structure (e.g. to model a bespoke as
          +k) instead of the tier value; UI shows "manual" vs "from tier"
          +.                                                             
      154  
      135 -### 6.6 Export                                                
      136 -- **FR-20** "Export JSON" — one file: contract JSON + resolved
          - SKU rate schedule + provenance + validation report + tool ver
          -sion + timestamp.                                             
      137 -- **FR-21** "Export CSV" — the SKU rate schedule as a flat tab
          -le (`sku_id, service, unit, list_price, discount_pct, effectiv
          -e_price, discount_source, account, effective_from, effective_t
          -o`) for direct load into a billing system.                    
      155 +### 6.6 Model-a-deal mode — what-if engine                    
      156 +- **FR-23** Build a *draft contract* from the chosen commitmen
          +t + resolved discount structure; run `resolve()` to get per-SK
          +U discounts.                                                  
      157 +- **FR-24** For each historical month line: `wouldPayUSD = lis
          +tAmountUSD × (1 − skuDiscountPct/100)`; `savingsVsPaidUSD = pa
          +idAmountUSD − wouldPayUSD`; also track `savingsVsListUSD = lis
          +tAmountUSD − wouldPayUSD`.                                    
      158 +- **FR-25** Aggregate: total list, total paid, total would-pay
          +, total savings (vs paid and vs list), savings %; per-month se
          +ries; SKUs ranked by savings.                                 
      159 +- **FR-26** **Commitment-fit:** `projectedAnnualUSD` = (sum of
          + `listAmountUSD` over the trailing window) × (12 / months in w
          +indow). If `projectedAnnualUSD ≥ commitment` → "usage clears t
          +he commitment; savings fully realized". Else `shortfallUSD = c
          +ommitment − projectedAnnualUSD`; show it and a `netFirstYearBe
          +nefitUSD = annualizedSavings − shortfallUSD` (labelled an esti
          +mate).                                                        
      160 +- **FR-27** All figures update live on commitment / override /
          + prospect change (< 50 ms).                                   
      161  
      162 +### 6.7 Model-a-deal mode — output                            
      163 +- **FR-28** Headline sentence: commitment, discount %, absolut
          +e savings, savings % over the window.                         
      164 +- **FR-29** Per-month chart (paid vs would-pay) and per-SKU sa
          +vings table (top N + "others").                               
      165 +- **FR-30** Commitment-fit callout with the shortfall / net-be
          +nefit figures when usage is below commitment.                 
      166 +- **FR-31** "Export savings summary" — JSON + a printable view
          + the customer can keep (inputs, tier, per-month table, totals,
          + the fit caveat).                                             
      167 +                                                              
      168 +### 6.8 Verification (shared)                                 
      169 +- **FR-32** `validate(...)` runs the rule catalog (§10) over w
          +hatever inputs the active mode has.                           
      170 +- **FR-33** Findings: `code`, `severity` (`ERROR`/`WARNING`/`I
          +NFO`), message, affected field(s)/SKU(s). Sorted ERROR → WARNI
          +NG → INFO.                                                    
      171 +- **FR-34** Export allowed with warnings; soft-gated with erro
          +rs (override possible).

      175  ## 7. Contract JSON schema (extraction target)
      176  
      177  ```jsonc
      178  {
      145 -  "accountNumber": string,               // e.g. "MER-772013";
          - expected pattern AAA-NNNNNN                                  
      179 +  "accountNumber": string,               // e.g. "MER-772013";
          + expected pattern ^[A-Z]{3}-\d{6}$                            
      180    "customerLegalName": string,
      181    "signedDate": ISODate | null,
      182    "currency": "USD" | "EUR" | "GBP",
      149 -  "term": {                                                   
      150 -    "startDate": ISODate | null,                              
      151 -    "endDate":   ISODate | null,                              
      152 -    "durationYears": number | null       // provided if explic
          -it dates are absent                                           
      153 -  },                                                          
      154 -  "annualCommitmentUSD": number,         // committed spend pe
          -r year                                                        
      183 +  "term": { "startDate": ISODate|null, "endDate": ISODate|null
          +, "durationYears": number|null },                             
      184 +  "annualCommitmentUSD": number,                              
      185  
      186    "discount": {
      157 -    "crossServicePct": number | null,    // baseline across al
          -l services, if any                                            
      158 -    "serviceOverrides": [                // service-level disc
          -ounts, if any                                                 
      159 -      { "service": string, "pct": number }                    
      160 -    ],                                                        
      161 -    "skuOverrides": [                    // rare: appendix/exh
          -ibit, exact SKU                                               
      162 -      { "skuId": string, "pct": number }                      
      163 -    ]                                                         
      187 +    "crossServicePct": number | null,                         
      188 +    "serviceOverrides": [ { "service": string, "pct": number }
          + ],                                                           
      189 +    "skuOverrides":     [ { "skuId": string,  "pct": number } 
          +]                                                             
      190    },
      191  
      166 -  "_provenance": {                       // per top-level fiel
          -d                                                             
      192 +  "_provenance": {                                            
      193      "<fieldPath>": { "sourceSnippet": string, "confidence": "h
           igh"|"medium"|"low" }
      194    }
      195  }
      196  ```
      197 +Valid iff at least one of `crossServicePct` / `serviceOverride
          +s` / `skuOverrides` is populated.                             
      198  
      172 -A valid contract has **at least one** of `crossServicePct`, `s
          -erviceOverrides`, `skuOverrides` populated.

          - 17.50,

          -2, "sku_override": 1, "none": 2 },

          +Tok / seats / 1k-calls)

          +e

          +tually paid (may reflect an existing discount)

      219 +- `listAmountUSD` is authoritative for the counterfactual; `pa
          +idAmountUSD` lets the tool show savings vs. what they pay *tod
          +ay* (and detect they're already on a discount).               
      220 +- Bundled prospect **Vantage Labs**: 6 months, growing trend, 
          +spread across API (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku, input+output+cache), Cla
          +ude Code, and 40–60 Enterprise seats. Trailing annualized ≈ $9
          +.4M. One prospect is enough for the demo; a second (**flat, sm

      224 +## 9. Commitment → discount tier table (bundled, illustrative)
      225  
      226 +```jsonc                                                      
      227 +[                                                             
      228 +  { "minAnnualUSD": 1000000,  "maxAnnualUSD": 3000000,  "label
          +": "Tier 1",                                                  
      229 +    "discount": { "crossServicePct": 10 } },                  
      230 +  { "minAnnualUSD": 3000000,  "maxAnnualUSD": 10000000, "label
          +": "Tier 2",                                                  
      231 +    "discount": { "crossServicePct": 15 } },                  
      232 +  { "minAnnualUSD": 10000000, "maxAnnualUSD": 25000000, "label
          +": "Tier 3",

      276  ## 11. Non-functional requirements
      277  
      255 -- **NFR-1 Self-contained.** Full evaluation with no API key, n
          -o network, no backend. Demo mode = bundled PDFs + pre-extracte
          -d JSON + bundled catalog.                                     
      256 -- **NFR-2 Deployable as static assets.** `vite build` → any st
          -atic host (GitHub Pages / Vercel / Netlify). No server, no env
          - vars required to run.                                        
      257 -- **NFR-3 Deterministic money math.** Prices held as decimal s
          -trings or integer minor units; `effectivePrice` rounding rule 
          -stated once (round half-up to 6 significant places / to the ce
          -nt for seats) and applied everywhere; CSV and JSON agree exact
          -ly.                                                           
      258 -- **NFR-4 Live feel.** Initial load < 2 s cold; full re-resolv
          -e + re-verify < 50 ms.                                        
      259 -- **NFR-5 Pure, unit-tested engine.** `resolve()` and `validat
          -e()` are framework-free pure functions with Vitest coverage: t
          -he rule catalog, the precedence table, and a full expected-sch
          -edule snapshot per sample contract.                           
      260 -- **NFR-6 Secret hygiene.** API key only in `localStorage`; on
          -ly ever sent to `api.anthropic.com`; never logged; never in an
          - export.                                                      
      261 -- **NFR-7 Legible under demo.** Keyboard-navigable forms, visi
          -ble focus, findings readable at a glance, SKU table sortable/f
          -ilterable by service and source.                              
      278 +- **NFR-1 Self-contained.** No API key, no network, no backend
          + needed for full evaluation. Demo mode = bundled PDFs + pre-ex
          +tracted JSON + bundled catalog + tier table + historical usage
          +.                                                             
      279 +- **NFR-2 Static deploy.** `vite build` → GitHub Pages / Verce
          +l / Netlify. No server, no required env vars.                 
      280 +- **NFR-3 Deterministic money math.** Prices/amounts as decima
          +l strings or integer minor units; one stated rounding rule (ha
          +lf-up; cents for seats/tools, 6 significant figures for per-MT
          +ok effective prices); CSV, JSON, and on-screen totals agree ex
          +actly.                                                        
      281 +- **NFR-4 Live feel.** Cold load < 2 s; full re-resolve + re-v
          +erify + re-compute savings < 50 ms.                           
      282 +- **NFR-5 Pure, unit-tested engine.** `resolve()`, `validate()
          +`, `whatIf()` are framework-free pure functions with Vitest co
          +verage: precedence table, rule catalog, a full expected SKU-sc
          +hedule snapshot per sample contract, and a savings-math reconc
          +iliation for the bundled prospect.                            
      283 +- **NFR-6 Secret hygiene.** API key only in `localStorage`; on
          +ly ever sent to `api.anthropic.com`; never logged; never expor
          +ted.                                                          
      284 +- **NFR-7 Legible under demo.** Keyboard-navigable, visible fo
          +cus, findings readable at a glance, SKU tables sortable/filter
          +able by service and source.

      366  ## 15. Open questions
      367  
      331 -1. **Cross-service applies to tools?** Does a "cross-service" 
          -discount cover **Server Tools** and **seats**, or only consump
          -tion (API + Claude Code)? *Leaning: cross-service means all SK
          -Us; a contract that wants to exclude a service says so with a 
          -0% override.*                                                 
      332 -2. **Account number format** — is `AAA-NNNNNN` the real patter
          -n? *Leaning: make the pattern a single configurable constant; 
          -treat a mismatch as ERROR for the demo.*                      
      333 -3. **Term from duration only** — if the PDF gives "3 years" an
          -d a start date but no end, tool derives `end = start + 3y − 1 
          -day`. If it gives duration and no start either, use `signedDat
          -e` as the start. OK? *Leaning: yes, and flag `LOW_CONFIDENCE` 
          -on the derived field.*                                        
      334 -4. **Effective-price rounding** — cent precision for seats/too
          -ls, 6 significant figures for per-MTok? Or hold discount % onl
          -y and let billing compute effective price? *Leaning: emit both
          - the discount % (authoritative) and a computed effective price
          - (convenience), with the rounding rule documented.*           
      335 -5. **Batch SKUs** — Batch API list price is already 50% of sta
          -ndard. Does a contract discount stack on top of the Batch pric
          -e, or off standard then Batch? *Leaning: contract discount app
          -lies to the Batch SKU's own list price (i.e. stacks); note it 
          -in the schedule.*                                             
      336 -6. **Multiple service overrides for the same service** — take 
          -the max, the last, or flag it? *Leaning: flag `DUPLICATE_SERVI
          -CE_OVERRIDE` (ERROR) rather than silently pick.*              
      368 +1. **Cross-service scope** — does a cross-service discount cov
          +er Server Tools and seats, or only consumption (API + Claude C
          +ode)? *Leaning: all SKUs; exclude a service via a 0% override.
          +*                                                             
      369 +2. **Account number format** — is `AAA-NNNNNN` real? *Leaning:
          + single configurable constant; mismatch = ERROR for the demo.*
      370 +3. **Term from duration only** — derive `end = start + Nyears 
          +− 1 day`; if no start either, use `signedDate`. Flag the deriv
          +ed field `LOW_CONFIDENCE`. OK?                                
      371 +4. **Effective-price rounding** — emit discount % (authoritati
          +ve) *and* a computed effective price (convenience), rounding r
          +ule documented. Agreed?                                       
      372 +5. **Batch SKUs** — contract discount applies to the Batch SKU
          +'s own (already-50%-off) list price, i.e. stacks. Agreed?     
      373 +6. **Savings baseline** — headline savings computed vs. `paidA
          +mountUSD` (what they pay today) or vs. `listAmountUSD`? *Leani
          +ng: vs. paid for the headline, show vs. list as a secondary fi
          +gure; if they're already fully at list, the two coincide.*    
      374 +7. **Projected annual** — straight annualization of the traili
          +ng window (× 12 / months). Good enough, or weight recent month
          +s more? *Leaning: straight, labelled; recency-weighting is a f
          +uture extension.*                                             
      375 +8. **Tier table values** — the §9 numbers are invented. Fine a
          +s clearly-labelled illustrative guidance for the prototype?   

⏺ Added the negotiation/savings capability. The doc is now one tool, two modes, 
  one shared engine:

  Model a deal (new — the sales/transparency tool)

  Inputs: a prospect's bundled historical usage table (6 months, by SKU:
  quantity, list amount, actually-paid amount) + a commitment slider.

  Engine: commitment → a bundled commitment→discount tier table (§9) picks a
  discount structure → the same resolve() runs it to per-SKU discounts → applied
  to each historical month.

  Output:
  - Headline: "$12M commit → 20% (+ Claude Code 25%) → saved $Z (P%) over 6 
    months"
  - Per-month chart: paid vs. would-have-paid
  - Per-SKU savings table
  - Commitment-fit callout (the honesty piece): if projected annual usage <
    commitment, show the shortfall true-up and net first-year benefit = 
    annualized savings − shortfall. New rules USAGE_BELOW_COMMITMENT,
    NET_BENEFIT_NEGATIVE, SHORT_HISTORY.
  - Export: printable savings summary the customer keeps.

  Demo prospect Vantage Labs: trailing annualized ≈ $9.4M, so dragging the
  slider to $12M deliberately trips the shortfall callout — shows the tool
  doesn't just oversell.

  New schemas / data

  - §8 historical usage schema + bundled Vantage Labs dataset
  - §9 commitment→discount tier table (4 tiers, labelled illustrative internal 
    guidance)
  - §6.5–6.7 functional reqs FR-18–FR-31 for the deal-modeling mode
  - §10 split the rule catalog into contract rules + what-if rules

  Scope reality check (§14)

  This is now two phases, not a 2–3h build:
  - Phase 1 — Onboard (≈2–3h): the assignment-complete slice — catalog,
    resolve(), validate(), 3 PDFs, SKU schedule + export
  - Phase 2 — Model a deal (≈+2h): tier table, usage data, whatIf(), slider,
    savings view

  Both share engine/. Recommendation: build Phase 1 end-to-end first.

  New open questions (§15)

  6. Savings headline baseline — vs. what they pay today (paidAmountUSD) or vs.
     list? I leaned "vs. paid for headline, vs. list as secondary".
  7. Projected annual — straight annualization (×12/months) vs.
     recency-weighted? I leaned straight + labelled.
  8. Tier table values are invented — OK as clearly-labelled illustrative
     guidance?

  Plus the 5 from before (cross-service scope, account format,
  term-from-duration, rounding, Batch stacking). Give me your calls and I'll
  start Phase 1.

⏺ Phase 1 is built, tested, and committed. Here's where things stand:

  What's done

  /Users/vksatish/anthropic-swe-takehome/ — a zero-build static web app (git
  repo initialized, one commit on main).

  ┌──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Piece   │                                                               │

  │          │ file://.)                                                     │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

  │          │ dependencies                                                  │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Catalog  │ js/catalog.js — ~25 SKUs across Claude API / Code / for Work  │
  │          │ / Server Tools, dated list-price book                         │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Samples  │ js/samples.js + agreements/*.pdf — Northwind (clean),         │
  │          │ Meridian (uncovered SKUs), Atlas (2 seeded errors)            │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │          │ node --test → 25 passing — precedence table, term derivation, │
  │ Tests    │  every validation rule, per-sample expected schedules,        │
  │          │ CSV/JSON export, determinism                                  │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Optional │ js/extract.js — browser → Anthropic API, PDF → contract JSON  │
  │          │ (needs a user key; samples work without)                      │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Deploy   │ .nojekyll + .github/workflows/pages.yml; also drag-drop to    │
  │          │ Netlify/Vercel                                                │
  └──────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Verified in-browser (Chrome)

  - All 3 samples load and resolve; Atlas shows the 2 errors + low-confidence
    warning, and editing the account number clears the error live
  - Three precedence levels visible in the SKU table (sku_override 30% /
    service:Claude Code 25% / cross_service 15%)
  - Meridian flags SKU_UNCOVERED for the two Server Tools SKUs
  - Product catalog drawer, discount-terms editing (add/remove overrides),
    source/service filters
  - Export CSV and Export JSON both download correct files (inspected the bundle
    — contract + provenance + schedule + validation report + timestamp)

⏺ It's open in your browser now (open index.html — runs straight from file://,
  no server needed). I couldn't drive it myself this round (the Chrome
  automation extension dropped its connection), so you run it — which is the
  real end-to-end test anyway.

  Full checklist with expected results at every step: docs/DEMO.md. The spine of
  it:

  1. Northwind (happy path) — every SKU resolves to 12% / cross-service;
  API-OPUS-5-INPUT List 5.00 → Eff. 4.4000. Export JSON + CSV both download.

  2. Meridian (leakage) — no baseline, so the two Server Tools SKUs come back as
  SKU_UNCOVERED (1 warning). Filter the table by source = uncovered to see
  exactly which.

  3. Atlas (2 seeded errors) —
  - atlas_9931 → ACCOUNT_NUMBER_MALFORMED
  - "Claude Enterprise Search" → UNKNOWN_SERVICE_IN_CONTRACT
  - derived end date → LOW_CONFIDENCE_FIELD
  - Fix the account number to ATL-009931 in column 2 → the error clears live, no
    reload. Delete the bad service row → second error clears.
  - Filter to opus-5-output: API-OPUS-5-OUTPUT is 30% / SKU override,
    API-OPUS-5-OUTPUT-BATCH is 15% / cross-service (discount stacks on the Batch
    list price).

  Engine tests: node --test → 25 pass.

  Live PDF extraction (optional): top-right API key → paste your key → Upload a 
  PDF… → pick agreements/atlas.pdf → Extract with Claude. Key stays in
  localStorage, never in exports.

  Tell me what breaks or feels off and I'll fix it.

✻ Brewed for 1m 17s

※ recap: We're building the Anthropic take-home: a zero-build billing-onboarding web app that resolves a signed contract's discounts down to product SKUs. ✻ Churned for 14s

❯ whats the workflow thats been built here                                      

⏺ The workflow: signed contract → billing-ready SKU rate schedule

  Who runs it: deal desk / billing operations, right after a committed-spend
  contract is signed, before the customer's usage starts getting invoiced.

  The problem it solves: the contract says things like "12% off across all 
  services" or "Claude Code 20%, Claude API 10%". The metered billing system
  doesn't understand "services" — it charges per SKU (API-OPUS-5-INPUT,
  CLAUDE-ENTERPRISE-SEAT, TOOL-WEB-SEARCH, …). Someone has to translate the
  first into the second, correctly, or the customer's first invoice is wrong.

  The five steps

  #: 1
  Step: Ingest the signed agreement
  In the tool: Pick a bundled sample, or upload the PDF and click Extract with 
  Claude
  What can go wrong (and gets caught): —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Step: Extract the terms
  In the tool: PDF → structured JSON: account number, term, annual commitment,
  currency, and the discount structure (cross-service / per-service / per-SKU).
   Every field carries a verbatim source snippet + confidence
  What can go wrong (and gets caught): Low-confidence fields flagged
  (LOW_CONFIDENCE_FIELD)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Step: Human review
  In the tool: Column 2 — every field editable, snippet on hover. The reviewer
  corrects the AI against the PDF
  What can go wrong (and gets caught): Malformed account number,
  invalid/underivable term, non-positive commitment, currency mismatch,
  duplicate/unknown service
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Step: Resolve to SKUs
  In the tool: The engine walks all ~25 catalog SKUs and assigns each one a
  discountPct + a source, using precedence: sku_override → service:<name> →
  cross_service → none
  What can go wrong (and gets caught): Contract names a service the catalog
  doesn't have (UNKNOWN_SERVICE_IN_CONTRACT); a SKU ends up with no rule and
  would bill at list (SKU_UNCOVERED); an override is worse than the baseline
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Step: Verify & export
  In the tool: Findings list (ERROR/WARNING/INFO), then Export JSON (full
  bundle:
  contract + provenance + schedule + validation report) or Export  CSV (flat
  sku_id, list_price, discount_pct, effective_price,  discount_source, … for
  the billing system to load)
  What can go wrong (and gets caught): Export is soft-gated while errors exist

  The loop

  Steps 3→4→5 recompute live on every edit. So the real workflow is: load
  contract → see errors → fix a field → watch the error clear and the SKU
  schedule update → repeat until the findings are clean → export. The Atlas
  sample is built to demonstrate exactly this (fix the account number, delete
  the bad service row, errors clear).

  What comes out

  A per-SKU rate schedule — for every product a customer could be metered on,
  the exact discount to apply and why (which contract clause it came from),
  scoped to the contract's account number and effective dates. That's the
  artifact the billing team consumes.

✻ Sautéed for 17s

❯ are we extracting from PDF?                                                   

⏺ Two paths, and they're different:

  Bundled samples (the default demo) — no. When you click Northwind / Meridian /
  Atlas, nothing parses the PDF. The contract JSON is hand-authored in
  js/samples.js (the expected field) — it's what extraction would produce. The
  PDF (agreements/*.pdf) is shown as text with an "Open PDF" link, but only for
  you to eyeball the extraction against. This is deliberate: the demo works with
  zero API key, zero network (requirement §11 NFR-1).

  Upload a PDF + "Extract with Claude" — yes, really. js/extract.js reads the
  uploaded file, base64-encodes it, and sends the actual PDF bytes to
  api.anthropic.com/v1/messages as a document block, with a prompt describing
  the target schema. Claude returns JSON, the app parses it into the form with
  per-field source snippets and confidence. This needs your Anthropic API key
  (set via the API key button).

  Caveat: I've not run the live extraction end-to-end — no key available in this
  session. The code path is written and syntax-checked, but untested against a
  real API response. The bundled-sample path is fully tested.

  Also note the bundled PDFs are synthetic — generated from the term-sheet text
  in samples.js by scripts/make-pdfs.mjs — so they exist mainly to give the
  upload path something realistic to run against, and to look like a real signed
  agreement.

✻ Cogitated for 1m 43s

❯ where do i get my api key                                                     

⏺ From the Anthropic Console:

  1. Go to https://console.anthropic.com/
  2. Sign in (or create an account)
  3. Settings → API keys → Create Key
  4. Copy it — it starts with sk-ant-... (you only see it once)

  Notes:
  - This is a Console API key, separate from your Claude Code login. It bills to
    that Console account/organization.
  - You need billing set up under Settings → Billing (add a payment method / buy
    credits) for calls to succeed — otherwise the extraction returns a 400/402
    and the app shows the error in the status line.
  - The key is stored only in your browser's localStorage, sent only to
    api.anthropic.com, and never written into any export. Clear it anytime via
    Set API key → Clear key.

  You don't need this for the core demo — all three bundled samples work with no
  key. It's only for extracting your own uploaded PDFs.

※ recap: Goal: build the Anthropic take-home — a committed-spend billing onboarding tool. Phase 1 (Onboard) is built, tested, and running: extracts a signed contract, resolves discounts to per-SKU rates, exports for billing. Next: you run the end-to-end test in the browser using docs/DEMO.md.

❯ in this mechanism we are creartig private SKU/Offer this will not be scalable 
  as we onboard more customers. we should have one public offers and based on   
  usage we should apply discounts latter                                        

⏺ You're right, and it's worth being precise about which part is the
  anti-pattern.

  What's actually the risk

  Not a per-customer SKU catalog — there's one shared catalog.js, and nothing
  mints new SKUs. skuOverrides references existing public SKU ids. resolve() is
  a pure function (publicCatalog, discountPolicy) → per-SKU discount.

  The risk is the output framing. The tool exports a "SKU rate schedule" with a
  materialized effectivePrice per SKU. If the billing system ingests that as its
  price list, you've forked the public catalog per customer: list-price changes
  don't propagate, you maintain N×M rows, snapshots go stale. That's the
  unscalable thing.

  The scalable shape

  - One public price book. Single source of truth for list prices.
  - Per-account discount policy — the durable onboarding artifact. Compact: { 
    crossServicePct, serviceOverrides[], skuOverrides[], effectiveFrom, 
    effectiveTo }. A few rows per customer, not a full catalog.
  - Rating engine applies the policy against the public price book at invoice 
    time. effectivePrice is computed on the fly, never stored per customer.
    List-price change → every customer picks it up automatically.
  - In this tool, the "SKU rate schedule" is then a dated preview for 
    verification (labeled with priceBookDate), not the system of record.

  "Apply discounts based on usage later"

  That's a second mechanism, and it also belongs at rating time, not onboarding:

  - Volume / commitment tiers in the policy — e.g. 0–1B tok: list · 1–5B: −15% ·
    5B+: −25%, evaluated each billing period against actual metered usage.
  - Period-end true-up / rebate credits for commitment attainment.

  Onboarding's job is to capture and validate those tier rules (contiguous, no
  gaps, non-overlapping); the rating engine evaluates them against usage.

  What I'd change in this repo

  1. Make "Export discount policy (JSON)" the primary export — compact,
     effective-dated.
  2. Relabel the SKU table + CSV as "Rate preview — priced at <priceBookDate>, 
     not the system of record."
  3. Add tiered-discount fields to the contract schema + TIER_GAP / TIER_OVERLAP
     validations.
  4. resolve() returns { policy, preview } separately.

  Want me to refactor Phase 1 around policy-as-artifact (plus usage tiers), or
  just do the relabel for now and keep tiers for Phase 2?

❯ new repo , new project . lets first talk about tech stack before              
  implemtnation and design it                                                   
⏺ Constraints I'm carrying in: it's a prototype, you want zero-build / easy to 
  unpack and run, self-contained so a reviewer runs it in seconds, and the
  workflow is more stateful than the last one (4-step wizard + form + document +
  signature + billing config).

  Here's the stack I'd propose, layer by layer:

  1. UI — the one real decision

  ┌──────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────┐
  │      Option      │              What               │     Trade-off      │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Alpine.js        │ x-data/x-show/x-model —         │ Tiny vendored dep; │
  │ (vendored,       │ purpose-built for multi-step    │  still opens from  │
  │ ~15KB, one file, │ forms & wizards, stays in HTML  │ file://            │
  │  no build)       │                                 │                    │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Pure vanilla     │                                 │ Zero deps, but the │
  │ (like the last   │ Hand-rolled DOM + a ~30-line    │  wizard state +    │
  │ prototype)       │ render/state helper             │ form binding gets  │
  │                  │                                 │ verbose fast       │
  └──────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────┘

  I lean Alpine this time — the wizard has enough state (step transitions, form
  fields, signed/unsigned, validation) that vanilla would be a lot of
  boilerplate. Still no npm install, still double-click-to-run.

  2. Logic — framework-free

  engine.js as a pure module: discount-policy resolution + validation + rate
  preview (ports cleanly from the last repo). Plain JS + JSDoc types. Tested
  with node --test (zero deps, worked well last time).

  catalog.js — the one shared public price book.

  3. PDF generation

  - Hand-rolled mini PDF writer (we have ~80 lines already) — dependency-free,
    fine for a text term-sheet. My pick.
  - or vendored jsPDF (~300KB) if we want nicer layout later.

  4. Persistence

  localStorage — an "onboarding record" object with a status (draft → sent → 
  signed → provisioned), plus a simple list view so you can have several in
  flight and resume one. No backend.

  5. Deploy

  Static files → open locally, or drop the folder on Netlify/GitHub Pages.

  ---

  Questions to lock it:
  1. Alpine, or stay pure vanilla?
  2. Mini PDF writer (no deps), or vendored jsPDF?
  3. localStorage + multi-onboarding list, or single in-memory onboarding
     (simpler, resets on refresh)?
  4. Repo name?

✻ Worked for 46s

❯ i want to make it easy for reviewer to just download the repo and try this by 
  itself with no setup requirement. they should be able to download the app     
  and get going                                                                 

⏺ That locks the stack. Here's the final call, optimized for "download → open →
  works":

  Stack (locked to zero-setup)

  Layer: UI
  Choice: Pure vanilla JS — no Alpine, no libraries
  Why it survives file://: Nothing to vendor, nothing to break. Classic <script>

  tags, not ES modules (modules are blocked from file://).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer: Data (catalog, doc template)
  Choice: Inline in .js files
  Why it survives file://: fetch() of local files is blocked from file://, so no

  external JSON.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer: PDF
  Choice: Hand-rolled mini writer (~80 lines, no dep)
  Why it survives file://: Pure JS. Blob + <a download> works from file:// in
  Chrome/Firefox.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer: State/persistence
  Choice: localStorage, with in-memory fallback + "export onboarding as JSON"
  Why it survives file://: Works from file://; the JSON export is the escape
  hatch if storage misbehaves.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer: Engine
  Choice: engine.js pure module, classic script + module.exports tail

  Choice: one styles.css via <link>
  Why it survives file://: Fine from file://.
