✻ Worked for 36s

❯ for backend code are we doing js ?                                            

⏺ No backend at all — that's the whole point of the zero-setup constraint.
  Everything runs in the browser.

  - No server, no Node at runtime, no database. Nothing listens on a port.
  - The "backend-ish" logic — discount resolution, validation, PDF generation,
    the onboarding state machine — is plain JS running client-side (engine.js,
    pdf.js, app.js).
  - "Persistence" = localStorage in the browser.
  - The only place Node shows up is the optional node --test for engine unit
    tests. Dev-time only; not needed to run the app.

  One deliberate choice: engine.js is written framework-free with no DOM/browser
  dependencies, so if this ever becomes a real system, that module lifts into a
  Node/whatever service unchanged. But for the prototype there's no server to
  write.

  If you're picturing a future real version (real DocuSign, real billing
  provisioning, multi-user), that's a separate stack conversation — not now.

✻ Brewed for 12s

❯ i want to make it statefull in the context of execution                       

⏺ "Stateful in the context of execution" — so each onboarding is a workflow 
  instance that carries its state as it moves through the steps, persists across
  reloads, and can be resumed exactly where it was. The UI step is derived from
  that state, not tracked separately.

  Here's the model I'd propose:

  The onboarding record (the state)

  {
    id,
    status,                    // the state-machine state
    agreement:   { customer, term, commitment, pricing… },   // Step 1 input
    document:    { generatedAt, snapshot },                   // Step 2 output
    signature:   { signerName, signedAt, mark },              // Step 3 output
    billingConfig: { discountPolicy, ratePreview, provisionedAt }, // Step 4
  output
    history: [ { from, to, at, action, note } ],   // transition log = the audit
  trail
    createdAt, updatedAt
  }

  The state machine

  DRAFT ──(agreement valid)──▶ AGREEMENT_READY ──(generate doc)──▶
  PENDING_SIGNATURE
    ▲                                                                    │
    └──────────────(revise, only before signature)──────────────────────┘
  PENDING_SIGNATURE ──(sign)──▶ SIGNED ──(provision billing)──▶ ACTIVE
  (any pre-SIGNED state) ──(void)──▶ CANCELLED
  Each transition has a guard (can't generate a doc from an invalid agreement;
  can't provision unless SIGNED). Transitions are a pure function apply(record, 
  action) → record in js/workflow.js, unit-tested — that's what makes "stateful
  execution" verifiable.

  Persistence

  localStorage: { onboardings: {id: record}, activeId }. Every transition writes
  through. Reload → rehydrate → land on the same step. A list view shows all
  onboardings with status badges; you can have several in flight.

  Decisions for you

  1. State representation: (a) mutable record + history[] log (lighter, my pick 
     for a prototype), or (b) event-sourced — append-only events, state = fold
     over them (more "properly stateful," replayable, more code).
  2. State set: the 6 above OK? Keep CANCELLED and the "revise before signature"
     back-edge, or trim for v1?
  3. Multiple concurrent onboardings + list view, or one at a time for the
     prototype?
  4. Workflow defined as a data config (states/transitions/guards as an object)
     so it's easy to extend later — agreed?

✻ Baked for 35s

❯ why not use python or kotlin?                                                 

⏺ Because of the constraint you set: download → open → works, no setup, no 
  server.

  The browser only executes JavaScript (or WASM). So:

  - Python doesn't run in a browser. To ship a Python app you need a running
    process — FastAPI / Flask / Streamlit — which means the reviewer runs pip 
    install + python app.py. That's a server and setup. (Pyodide/WASM avoids the
    server but is a ~10MB download, slow cold start, and still renders through
    JS/DOM.)
  - Kotlin/JS compiles to JavaScript, but via Gradle — the reviewer downloads
    source that won't run until ./gradlew build. Kotlin/JVM or /Native → a
    server or a desktop binary → setup. The only way to make Kotlin "open and
    run" is to commit the compiled JS, at which point the shipped artifact is JS
    anyway and you've added a toolchain to maintain.

  Either one reintroduces exactly the install/build/run step you've said three
  times you don't want.

  Where they'd be the right call: if we had a real backend (billing
  provisioning, real DocuSign, multi-user, a database) — FastAPI or Ktor/Spring
  are excellent there. Or if this were a CLI (Python) or an Android app
  (Kotlin).

  So the real fork is:

  ┌──────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │              │ Zero-setup prototype  │       "Real architecture"        │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Frontend     │ vanilla JS, static    │ JS/React, static                 │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Backend      │ none (browser-only)   │ Python (FastAPI) or Kotlin       │
  │              │                       │ (Ktor)                           │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Reviewer     │ double-click          │ docker compose up / uvicorn      │
  │ runs         │ index.html            │ app:main                         │
  └──────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  You've been pushing hard toward column 1. If you'd rather show a backend and
  are OK with the reviewer running one command, say so and we'll design it in
  Python or Kotlin. Which column?

✻ Cogitated for 34s

❯ how about if we do this in kotlin                                             

⏺ Kotlin's a fine language for this domain — its sealed classes + exhaustive 
  when model a state machine (states, transitions, guards) better than Python or
  JS do. But as a delivery vehicle for "reviewer downloads and tries it fast,"
  it's the heaviest of the three:

  ┌────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Friction  │                           Detail                           │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ JDK prereq │ Reviewer needs JDK 17+ installed (heavier than Python,     │
  │            │ which ships on macOS/Linux, or a browser).                 │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Gradle     │ First ./gradlew run downloads the Gradle distribution +    │
  │ cold start │ deps — minutes on a fresh machine. Opposite of "get        │
  │            │ going."                                                    │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │            │ No free one-click host like Streamlit Cloud. You'd deploy  │
  │ Hosting    │ a JVM app to Railway/Render/Fly with a Dockerfile — real   │
  │            │ work to hand the Anthropic team a live URL.                │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Ceremony   │ Ktor is lean-ish, but you write routing + HTML DSL +       │
  │            │ wiring — more code than Streamlit for the same wizard.     │
  └────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  If you go Kotlin anyway, the stack would be:
  - Ktor + kotlinx.html server-rendered pages (or htmx for the step transitions)
  - Workflow.kt — sealed-class state machine, the centerpiece, unit-tested with
    kotest/JUnit
  - Engine.kt — pricing resolution
  - SQLite via Exposed or plain JDBC
  - OpenPDF for the agreement PDF
  - Run: ./gradlew run; deploy: Dockerfile → Fly.io/Render

  Ranked for this assignment's constraints (deployed, use immediately, scoping 
  graded):
  1. Vanilla JS static — lightest, free host (you rejected it)
  2. Python + Streamlit — one pip install, free one-click host, least code
  3. Kotlin + Ktor — best type-safe state machine, heaviest to run and host

  The one question that settles this: what's your deciding criterion — best fit 
  for the task, or the language you want to show fluency in? The assignment
  explicitly says "use any language you're comfortable with," so if Kotlin is
  your strongest, that's a legitimate reason to eat the heavier run/host story.

❯  private-pricing-customer-onboarding and persistance is okay. lets do setup

⏺ Repo: private-pricing-customer-onboarding. Persistence approved. Designing
  Step 1 — Author agreement (the Sales screen). No code yet.

  What Sales sees

  A wizard with a progress bar: Draft ▸ Agreement ▸ Signature ▸ Billing. A
  sidebar lists existing onboardings (resume/switch) + "New onboarding". Step 1
  is a grouped form:

  A · Customer & deal
  - Customer legal name (required)
  - Sales owner (your name — goes in the signature block + audit log)
  - CRM opportunity ID (optional, free text)

  B · Term
  - Start date (required)
  - Term length: 1 yr / 2 yr / 3 yr / custom → end date derived and shown

  C · Commitment
  - Annual committed spend, USD (required, > 0)

  D · Pricing (the core)
  - Discount model: Cross-service · Per-service · Both (baseline + overrides)
  - Cross-service %: one number
  - Per-service: repeatable rows — [service ▾ from catalog] [%] [＋/✕]
  - Live coverage helper: as they type — "covers 23 of 25 SKUs; Server Tools 
    will bill at list price." Pulled from engine.py. This is what makes it a
    tool, not a form.

  E · Notes (optional free text — special terms for legal/reviewer)

  Actions
  - Save draft → persists in DRAFT, stays on screen
  - Generate agreement → → runs the guard (required fields, % in 0–100, ≥1
    discount, valid term, commitment > 0, known services); if clean, transitions
    DRAFT → AGREEMENT_READY and advances to Step 2. Coverage gaps are a
    warning, not a block.

  Decisions for you

  1. Account number — Sales enters it here, or the system assigns it at billing
     setup (Step 4)? (I lean: assigned at Step 4 — Sales is onboarding a 
     brand-new customer.)
  2. Pricing scope for v1 — cross-service % + per-service % only, or also
     per-SKU exhibit overrides? (I lean: defer per-SKU.)
  3. Currency + billing frequency — fix to USD / monthly-in-arrears for the
     prototype, or make them fields?
  4. Live coverage helper in Step 1 — include it, or keep Step 1 a plain form
     and show coverage only at billing setup? (I lean: include a light version.)

  │ Term length │ 12 / 24 / 36 months → end date shown (derived) │
  └─────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Commitment

  ┌──────────────────────────────┬─────────────┐
  │            Field             │    Notes    │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Annual committed spend (USD) │ number, > 0 │
  └──────────────────────────────┴─────────────┘

  Pricing

  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Field       │                    Notes                     │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Discount model   │ Cross-service / Per-service / Both           │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cross-service %  │ shown for Cross-service or Both              │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Per-service rows │ service ▾ + %, shown for Per-service or Both │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Coverage helper  │ live text — "covers 23/25 SKUs…"             │
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Fixed / not shown (stamped by the system)

  Currency = USD · billing = monthly in arrears · sales owner = stamped default
  · account number = assigned at Step 4 · ID, status, timestamps, history.

  That's 6 inputs + the pricing block.

  One more trim question: for a true minimal v1, do you want pricing to be just 
  a single "Cross-service discount %" field (drop per-service until later), or
  keep the 3-way model? A single % keeps Step 1 tiny and still feeds the per-SKU
  resolution + coverage helper.

✻ Brewed for 24s

❯ for per service give two options . Service specific discount or servivce      
  specific rate where user has ablity to enter flat rate or discount . i dont   
  want coverage helper                                                          

⏺ Updated. Coverage helper removed. Pricing block now:

  Pricing

  Discount model: Cross-service · Per-service · Both

  Cross-service (for Cross-service or Both)
  - Cross-service discount % — one number, 0–100

  Per-service rows (for Per-service or Both) — each row:

  ┌─────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         │                                                   │
  ├─────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Service │ dropdown from catalog                             │
  ├─────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Type    │ Discount % or Flat rate                           │
  ├─────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Value   │ if Discount: 0–100  ·  if Flat rate: $ amount ≥ 0 │
  └─────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  So a row is e.g. Claude Code · Discount % · 20 or Claude Code · Flat rate · 
  $4.50.

  How it resolves (for later, not building now)

  Per-service entry becomes { service, type: "discount" | "rate", value }. At
  billing setup:
  - discount → that service's SKUs get value % off list
  - rate → that service's SKUs get effectivePrice = value (flat, replaces list)
  - Precedence unchanged: per-service override (discount or rate) beats
    cross-service.

  One clarification

  A flat rate is one number applied to every SKU in that service. That's clean
  for single-SKU services (Claude Code seat, a tool) but blunt for multi-SKU
  ones like Claude API (input vs output vs cache are very different prices). For
  the prototype, do we:
  - (a) allow it anywhere, applied bluntly to all SKUs in the service, or
  - (b) only offer "Flat rate" when the chosen service has a single SKU
    (Discount % only for multi-SKU services)?

  - Name — pre-filled random John Doe / Jane Doe, editable
  - Company name — required

  Term
  - Start date — default today
  - Term length — 12 / 24 / 36 months → end date derived, shown

  Commitment
  - Annual committed spend (USD) — > 0

  Pricing
  - Discount model — Cross-service / Per-service / Both
  - Cross-service % — 0–100 (Cross-service or Both)

  Stamped by system (not shown): currency = USD · billing = monthly in arrears ·
  sales owner = default · account number = assigned at Step 4 · ID, status,
  timestamps, history.

⏺ Auto mode lets Claude handle permission prompts automatically — Claude
  checks each tool call for risky actions and prompt injection before 
  executing. Actions Claude identifies as safe are executed, while 
  actions Claude identifies as risky are blocked and Claude may try a 
  different approach. Ideal for long-running tasks. Claude can make 
  mistakes that allow harmful commands to run, it's recommended to only 
  use in isolated environments. Shift+Tab to change mode.

  → Pricing Schedule two-column table (Term | Value with all the discount rows)
  → 8 boilerplate legal clauses → Signatures. Percentages show as 20%, no
  decimals. The progress tracker is now a green-filling stepper with emoji
  icons, no strikethrough. Let me tighten the PDF so the footer stops orphaning
  onto a 3rd page.

⏺ Both changes are in (committed b495580). Verified end-to-end in the browser.

  Agreement PDF — redesigned

  1. Parties — Anthropic, PBC + "Represented by: ____ (Anthropic Authorized
     Signatory)" and the customer company + "Represented by: {signer name}".
  2. Pricing Schedule — a two-column Term | Value table:
     Effective date · Contract term · End date · Currency · Billing · Annual 
     committed spend · Total committed value · Discount model · Cross-service 
     discount · {Service} discount (one row per per-service discount).
  3. Terms and Conditions — 8 numbered boilerplate clauses that read like a real
     agreement: Effective Date & Term, Committed Spend, Pricing & Discounts,
     Billing & Payment, Confidentiality, Termination, Entire Agreement,
     Counterparts & Electronic Signature.
  4. Signatures — "For Anthropic, PBC" / "For {Company}" blocks; the customer
     side is filled in (name + date) once signed.

  Fits 2 pages across all discount models. Percentages have no decimals — 20%,
  everywhere (PDF, the form inputs, and the on-screen preview).

  Step tracker — pizza-tracker style

  Replaced the strikethrough list with a filling stepper:
  - 📝 Draft · 📄 Agreement · 🖊️ Signature · 🎉 Signed — emoji in circles
  - completed steps: solid green circle with ✓, green connector line
  - current step: amber ring
  - upcoming: grey outline
  - no strikethrough

  Used inline HTML/CSS (no image assets). Real images would need bundling; the
  emoji version keeps it zero-asset.

  Tests still 14/14. To see it: cd ~/private-pricing-customer-onboarding && 
  .venv/bin/streamlit run app.py, click through New onboarding → fill company +
  spend → Generate → Send → Mark signed, and download the PDF at any post-draft

❯ now next step lets do billing configuration setup in our private pricing      
  table                                                                         

⏺ Designing the billing configuration step (SIGNED → ACTIVE). No code yet —
  let's align.

  What it does

  Once signed, resolve the agreement's discount policy against the one public 
  catalog into a private pricing table — a discount + effective price for every
  SKU — then review and provision.

  SIGNED ──[review private pricing table]──▶ provision_billing ──▶ ACTIVE

  The engine (engine.py, pure + tested)

  resolve(agreement, catalog) -> list[rate_line], one line per catalog SKU:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┐
  │              field               │                                      │
  ├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ sku_id, service, unit,           │ from catalog                         │
  │ list_price                       │                                      │
  ├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ discount_pct                     │ resolved                             │
  ├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ effective_price                  │ list_price × (1 − pct/100), rounded  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ source                           │ service:<name> / cross_service /     │
  │                                  │ list                                 │
  └──────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┘

  Precedence (highest wins): per-service discount → cross-service discount →
  list price (0%). (Per-SKU overrides deferred.)

  The screen

  - SIGNED screen grows a "Billing configuration" section: the private pricing
    table (grouped by service, sortable), a small summary ("23 of 25 SKUs
    discounted; 2 at list price"), effective dates = the agreement term, and a
    Provision billing account button.
  - ACTIVE screen (new): "Provisioned {date} to account {number}", the final
    table, and Export (JSON + CSV).

  Stored on the record (billing_config)

  { account_number, effective_from, effective_to, price_book_date,
    policy: {cross_service_pct, per_service[]},   # the compact durable artifact
    rate_table: [ ...resolved lines... ],         # snapshot for review/export
    provisioned_at }

  Tracker

  Add a 5th step: 📝 Draft · 📄 Agreement · 🖊️ Signature · ✅ Signed · 💳 
  Billing.

  Questions

  1. Review-only, or editable? I propose read-only auto-resolution in v1 — no
     per-SKU tweaks. OK?
  2. Uncovered SKUs — show a note flagging SKUs that land at list price (so
     billing isn't surprised)? You didn't want a coverage helper on the Step 1
     form, but here it seems useful. Include it?
  3. Export — JSON + CSV, or JSON only for the prototype?
  4. Guard on provision_billing — require status SIGNED + account number present
     + at least one discounted SKU. Anything else?
  5. price_book_date — stamp it from catalog.py so the snapshot is dated.
     Agreed?

✻ Baked for 33s

❯ the way i want to store inforemation is in the form of Jason something like   
  this. for service specicic discount i want to capture it as . the billing     
  confiration for ssd in the form of expression so that we can user it filter   
  against product catalog to identify which skus quailify for discount. lets    
  first come with sample json before we go and implement                        

⏺ Here's a proposed sample. The key idea per your ask: service-specific 
  discounts are stored as rules with a match expression that the billing/rating
  engine filters the catalog with — SKUs aren't hard-listed.

  {
    "kind": "private-pricing-billing-config",
    "version": "1.0",
    "onboarding_ref": "ONB-5B70CDD2",
    "account_number": "GLBX-004417",
    "customer": { "company_name": "Globex Corporation", "signer_name": "Jane 
  Doe" },
    "currency": "USD",
    "billing": "monthly in arrears",
    "effective_from": "2026-09-05",
    "effective_to": "2027-09-04",
    "annual_committed_spend_usd": 5000000,
    "price_book_date": "2026-08-01",
    "provisioned_at": "2026-09-06T00:54:46+00:00",

    "discount_rules": [
      {
        "id": "ssd-claude-code",
        "type": "service_specific_discount",
        "description": "Claude Code service discount",
        "priority": 10,
        "discount_pct": 20,
        "match": { "all": [ { "field": "service", "op": "eq", "value": "Claude 
  Code" } ] }
      },
      {
        "id": "ssd-claude-for-work",
        "type": "service_specific_discount",
        "description": "Claude for Work service discount",
        "priority": 10,
        "discount_pct": 15,
        "match": { "all": [ { "field": "service", "op": "eq", "value": "Claude 
  for Work" } ] }
      },
      {
        "id": "csd-baseline",
        "type": "cross_service_discount",
        "description": "Cross-service baseline discount",
        "priority": 100,
        "discount_pct": 12,
        "match": { "all_skus": true }
      }
    ],

    "resolution_note": "For each SKU in the public catalog, evaluate 
  discount_rules in ascending priority; the first rule whose `match` expression 
  is satisfied applies its discount_pct. SKUs matched by no rule bill at list 
  price.",

    "rate_table_snapshot": {
      "generated_at": "2026-09-06T00:54:46+00:00",
      "authoritative": false,
      "lines": [
        { "sku_id": "API-OPUS-5-INPUT", "service": "Claude API", "unit":
  "per_mtok",
          "list_price": 5.0, "applied_rule": "csd-baseline", "discount_pct": 12,
  "effective_price": 4.4 },
        { "sku_id": "CLAUDE-CODE-USAGE", "service": "Claude Code", "unit":
  "per_mtok",
          "list_price": 6.0, "applied_rule": "ssd-claude-code", "discount_pct":
  20, "effective_price": 4.8 },
        { "sku_id": "TOOL-WEB-SEARCH", "service": "Server Tools", "unit":
  "per_1k_calls",
          "list_price": 10.0, "applied_rule": null, "discount_pct": 0,
  "effective_price": 10.0 }
      ],
      "summary": { "skus_total": 25, "skus_discounted": 23, "skus_at_list": 2,
                   "skus_at_list_ids": ["TOOL-WEB-SEARCH", "TOOL-CODE-EXEC"] }
    }
  }

  The match expression — a structured predicate so no string parser / eval is
  needed:
  - { "field": <catalog field>, "op": "eq" | "in", "value": ... } — leaf
  - { "all": [ ...leaves... ] } — AND · { "any": [...] } — OR · { "all_skus": 
    true } — cross-service (matches everything)
  - Catalog fields available to field: id, service, unit, price_source

  Decisions

  1. Expression form — this structured predicate, or a string DSL like "service 
     == 'Claude Code'" (needs a small safe evaluator)? I recommend the 
     structured form for the prototype.
  2. Cross-service — same rule model with { "all_skus": true } (as above), or a
     separate top-level field? I recommend keeping one rule model.
  3. rate_table_snapshot — include it (labeled authoritative: false) for
     review/audit, or store only the rules and never materialize? I recommend 
     include-but-non-authoritative.
  4. Precedence — explicit priority int (lower wins), or array order? I 
     recommend explicit priority.

❯ why do we have rate_table_snapshot here ?                                     

⏺ You're right — it shouldn't be there. It's the exact thing you flagged
  earlier: a materialized per-customer price list that goes stale when list
  prices change, and it duplicates what discount_rules + the catalog already
  determine.

  Drop it. The stored billing config is just:
  - metadata (account, dates, currency, commitment, price_book_date,
    provisioned_at)
  - discount_rules (with the match expressions)

  The private pricing table shown on the billing screen — and the CSV export, if
  we keep one — is derived on the fly by running the resolver against the
  current catalog. Never persisted.

  (If an audit trail of "rates as of provisioning" is ever wanted, that's a
  separate dated export artifact, not part of the live config.)

  So the config JSON becomes:

  {

     - match_sku(expr, sku) — evaluates {field, op: eq|in, value}, {all:[…]},
       {any:[…]}, {all_skus:true}
     - build_billing_config(record) — agreement → the JSON above (cross-service
       → one csd-* rule; each per-service row → one ssd-* rule)
     - resolve_rate_table(config, catalog) — per SKU: first matching
       service_specific_discount, else matching cross_service_discount, else
       list; returns lines + a summary (discounted / at-list counts). Derived, 
       never stored.

     SIGNED + account number present; stores billing_config (the JSON) on the
     record.

     (derived rate table grouped by service + at-list note) with a Provision 
     billing account button; new ACTIVE screen shows the config + Export JSON
     and Export CSV (CSV = the derived table); tracker gains a 5th step 💳 
     Billing.
  5. tests/ — match_sku cases, resolve_rate_table precedence + at-list,
     build_billing_config shape, the provision_billing transition.

  Proceed?

  {

    "onboarding_ref": "ONB-5B70CDD2",
    "account_number": "GLBX-004417",
    "customer": { "company_name": "Globex Corporation", "signer_name": "Jane 
  Doe" },

    "cross_service_discount_pct": 12,

    "discount_rules": [
      { "id": "ssd-claude-code", "type": "service_specific_discount",
        "description": "Claude Code service discount", "discount_pct": 20,
        "match": { "all": [ { "field": "service_code", "op": "eq", "value":
  "CLAUDE_CODE" } ] } },
      { "id": "ssd-claude-for-work", "type": "service_specific_discount",
        "description": "Claude for Work service discount", "discount_pct": 15,
        "match": { "all": [ { "field": "service_code", "op": "eq", "value":
  "CLAUDE_FOR_WORK" } ] } }
    ]
  }

  - cross_service_discount_pct — flat number, or null if the agreement has no
    baseline.
  - discount_rules — SSD only; match evaluator now just needs {field, op: eq|in,
    value} + all / any (no more all_skus).
  - Resolution per SKU: first matching discount_rules entry → else
    cross_service_discount_pct (if set) → else list price.

  Same implementation plan as before, minus all_skus. Proceed?

  {
    "kind": "private-pricing-billing-config",
    "cross_service_discount_pct": 12,

      { "id": "ssd-claude-code", "type": "service_specific_discount",

  What's in

  Piece: catalog.py
  SERVICE_CODES enum + service_code on every SKU; a CATALOG dict
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: engine.py (new, pure)
  match_sku (eq/in/all/any) · build_billing_config · resolve_rate_table
  (derived,
  never stored) · rate_table_to_csv
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: workflow.py
  ACTIVE status · provision_billing transition (guard: SIGNED + account number)
  ·
  billing_config on the record
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: app.py
  SIGNED screen → "5 · Billing configuration": the resolved private pricing
  table
  + discount summary + "💳 Provision billing account". New ACTIVE screen: the
  table + Export JSON / Export CSV + a config-JSON expander. Tracker now 5
  steps ending 💳 Billing.

  Resolution per SKU: first matching discount_rules entry → else
  cross_service_discount_pct → else list price. SKUs matched by nothing are
  flagged as "at list price" in the summary.

  Test it:
  cd ~/private-pricing-customer-onboarding && .venv/bin/streamlit run app.py
  New onboarding → fill through to Mark as signed → the billing table appears →
  Provision billing account → ACTIVE screen with the table and JSON/CSV
  downloads.

  invovice that month                                                           

⏺ Designing an invoicing view on the ACTIVE screen. No code yet — let's align.

  What it does

  For an ACTIVE account: pick a billing month, enter usage per SKU, and generate
  that month's invoice using the billing config's resolved rates.

  Proposed design

  Where: the ACTIVE screen gets tabs — Rate table (what's there now) |
  Invoicing.

  Invoicing tab:
  1. Billing month picker (YYYY-MM).
  2. Usage entry — an editable table of the catalog SKUs: SKU · service · unit ·
     unit price (readonly) · quantity (editable, default 0). Plus a Load sample
     usage button that fills plausible quantities for a one-click demo.
  3. Generate invoice for {month} → builds the invoice from rows with quantity >
     0.
  4. A list of generated invoices (one per month), each re-openable with a
     download.

  Engine (engine.py, pure):
  build_invoice(config, catalog, month, usage, issued_at) -> invoice — resolves
  each used SKU's rate from the config, amount = quantity × unit_price, sums to
  a total.

  Invoice JSON:
  {
    "kind": "private-pricing-invoice", "version": "1.0",
    "invoice_number": "INV-GLBX-004417-2026-09",
    "account_number": "GLBX-004417",
    "billing_period": "2026-09",
    "issued_at": "...", "currency": "USD", "price_book_date": "2026-08-01",
    "lines": [
      { "sku_id": "API-OPUS-5-INPUT", "description": "...", "unit": "per_mtok",
        "quantity": 1200.0, "list_price": 5.0, "discount_pct": 12,
        "unit_price": 4.4, "amount": 5280.0 }
    ],
    "subtotal": 5280.0, "total": 5280.0
  }

  Storage: record["invoices"] — a list, one per month. Routed through
  workflow.apply(rec, "record_invoice", ...) so it's in the history log; status
  stays ACTIVE.

  Decisions

  1. Invoice = simple metered total (Σ quantity × effective price). No
     commitment drawdown / true-up / overage for now — agreed?
  2. Usage entry — the editable full-catalog table + "load sample usage" button,
     or repeatable "pick SKU + quantity" rows? (I lean editable table.)
  3. Invoice artifact — on-screen + PDF (styled like the agreement) + CSV? Or
     on-screen + JSON only?
  4. One invoice per month — block generating a second for the same month
     (overwrite with confirmation), or allow duplicates?
  5. Show a "savings vs list price" figure on the invoice (value story), or keep
     it plain?

✻ Cogitated for 44s

❯ 1. yes , 2. yes 3. pdf with gross price and net price post discoiint 4. yes

      215 +def build_invoice(config: dict, month: str, usage: dict, catal
          +og: dict | None = None) -> dict:                              
      216 +    """Config + one month's usage -> an invoice (issued_at lef
          +t null).                                                      
      217 +                                                              
      218 +    Simple metered billing: amount = quantity x net unit price
          +. Each line also                                              
      219 +    carries the gross (list) unit price and amount so the invo
          +ice shows both.                                               
      220 +    No commitment drawdown / true-up / overage.               
      221 +    """                                                       
      222 +    catalog = catalog or CATALOG                              
      223 +    rates = {ln["sku_id"]: ln for ln in resolve_rate_table(con
          +fig, catalog)["lines"]}                                       
      224 +    by_id = {s["id"]: s for s in catalog["skus"]}             
      225 +                                                              
      226 +    lines: list[dict] = []                                    
      227 +    gross_subtotal = 0.0                                      
      228 +    net_subtotal = 0.0                                        
      229 +    for sku_id, qty in usage.items():                         
      230 +        qty = float(qty or 0)                                 
      231 +        if qty <= 0 or sku_id not in rates:                   
      232 +            continue                                          
      233 +        r = rates[sku_id]                                     
      234 +        gross_amount = round(qty * r["list_price"], 2)        
      235 +        net_amount = round(qty * r["effective_price"], 2)     
      236 +        gross_subtotal += gross_amount                        
      237 +        net_subtotal += net_amount                            
      238 +        lines.append({                                        
      239 +            "sku_id": sku_id,                                 
      240 +            "description": by_id[sku_id]["display_name"],     
      241 +            "unit": r["unit"],                                
      242 +            "quantity": qty,                                  
      243 +            "list_price": r["list_price"],                    
      244 +            "discount_pct": r["discount_pct"],                
      245 +            "unit_price": r["effective_price"],               
      246 +            "gross_amount": gross_amount,                     
      247 +            "amount": net_amount,                             
      248 +        })                                                    
      249 +                                                              
      250 +    lines.sort(key=lambda ln: ln["sku_id"])                   
      251 +    gross_subtotal = round(gross_subtotal, 2)                 
      252 +    net_subtotal = round(net_subtotal, 2)                     
      253 +                                                              
      254 +    return {                                                  
      255 +        "kind": INVOICE_KIND,                                 
      256 +        "version": CONFIG_VERSION,                            
      257 +        "invoice_number": f"INV-{config['account_number']}-{mo
          +nth}",                                                        
      258 +        "account_number": config["account_number"],           
      259 +        "customer": dict(config["customer"]),                 
      260 +        "config_ref": config["onboarding_ref"],               
      261 +        "billing_period": month,                              
      262 +        "issued_at": None,                                    
      263 +        "currency": config["currency"],                       
      264 +        "price_book_date": config["price_book_date"],         
      265 +        "lines": lines,                                       
      266 +        "gross_subtotal": gross_subtotal,                     
      267 +        "discount_total": round(gross_subtotal - net_subtotal,
          + 2),                                                          
      268 +        "subtotal": net_subtotal,                             
      269 +        "total": net_subtotal,                                
      270 +    }                                                         
      271 +                                                              
      272 +                                                              
      273 +def stamp_invoice_issued(invoice: dict) -> dict:              
      274 +    invoice = dict(invoice)                                   
      275 +    invoice["issued_at"] = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoforma
          +t(timespec="seconds")                                         
      276 +    return invoice

⏺ Committed 46db37d. 33 tests pass. Verified the engine, PDF, and workflow via
  tests + direct runs (couldn't screenshot — Chrome extension still down).

  What's in

  ACTIVE screen now has tabs: "Rate table" | "Invoicing".

  Invoicing tab:
  - Billing month input (YYYY-MM, defaults to current month)
  - Load sample usage button → fills plausible quantities
  - Editable table of all catalog SKUs: SKU · service · unit · net $/unit 
    (read-only) · quantity
  - Generate invoice for {month} → builds the invoice from rows with qty > 0
  - Below: each generated invoice in an expander with line detail + ⬇ Invoice 
    PDF

  engine.build_invoice — simple metered: amount = quantity × net unit price.
  Each line also carries list_price and gross_amount. Invoice totals:
  gross_subtotal, discount_total, subtotal/total (net). No
  drawdown/true-up/overage.

  Invoice PDF — one page: header (invoice #, account, period, issued, terms),
  line table with Item · Qty · List $/u · Disc · Net $/u · Amount, totals block
  Gross subtotal / Discount (N%) / Net total due.

  workflow.record_invoice — guarded mutation, status stays ACTIVE. One invoice
  per month; regenerating replaces it; logged in history.

  Manual test

  cd ~/private-pricing-customer-onboarding && .venv/bin/streamlit run app.py
  New onboarding → fill through → Generate → Send → Mark signed → Provision 
  billing account → on the ACTIVE screen open Invoicing → Load sample usage →
  Generate invoice → open the invoice expander → Invoice PDF.

❯ whats the best experiance to how invocing .Should it be called current usage  
  report ?                                                                      

⏺ It's a preview, not an invoice — the usage is hand-entered, not metered, and
  Sales (not finance) is the user. Calling it "Invoice" oversells it; "Current
  usage report" is closer but "current" implies live data it doesn't have.

  What this artifact actually is

  Given a month's usage, show what it costs at this account's negotiated rates 
  vs. list price. Its value for Sales is the comparison — "here's what your
  agreement saves you."

  Naming options

  ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │        Name        │                        Fit                         │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Billing preview    │ Honest — it's an estimate from entered usage. My   │
  │                    │ pick.                                              │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Monthly cost       │ Same idea, more explicit.                          │
  │ preview            │                                                    │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Current usage      │ "Current" implies live metering; misleading here.  │
  │ report             │                                                    │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Invoice            │ Reserved for the finance system-of-record          │
  │                    │ document.                                          │
  └────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Best experience

  1. Tab name: "Billing preview" (or "Preview a month's bill").
  2. Lead with the value. After generating, a headline row:
     *"September 2026 — list price $5,850 · your rate $5,067 · saves $783 
     (13%)"*
  3. Live total while entering usage — running net total updates as quantities
     change, so it feels like a calculator, not a form.
  4. PDF titled "Billing Preview Statement" (not "Invoice"), same gross/net line
     detail. Keeps it clearly non-binding.
  5. Keep "Load sample usage" for the demo.

  Question

  Do you want this to stay a preview/estimate (Sales-facing, non-binding,
  emphasises savings), or should it actually be "the invoice" — the document
  you'd hand a customer? That decides the name and whether we show the savings
  framing or keep it a plain bill.

✻ Cooked for 39s

  consistency matters) and add the experience pieces: a savings headline, a live
  running total while you enter usage, and a preview-flavoured PDF.

⏺ Write(docs/REQUIREMENTS.md)

        1 -# Requirements & design notes                                 
        1 +# Requirements — Private Pricing Customer Onboarding          
        2  
        3 -## Purpose                                                    
        3 +## 1. Overview                                                
        4  
        5 -Onboard a customer onto a **private (committed-spend) pricing 
          -agreement**. Target                                           
        6 -user: **Sales**, doing the onboarding themselves — authoring d
          -eal terms, producing                                          
        7 -a signable agreement, and (after signature) handing a billing-
          -ready configuration                                           
        8 -to the billing team.                                          
        5 +A web tool for a Sales user to onboard a customer onto a priva
          +te (committed-spend)                                          
        6 +pricing agreement, and to run that agreement afterward. The us
          +er authors the deal                                           
        7 +terms, the tool produces a signable agreement PDF, the custome
          +r signs (mocked),                                             
        8 +the tool compiles a billing configuration, and the user genera
          +tes a monthly                                                 
        9 +billing preview from usage.                                   
       10  
       10 -## Principles                                                 
       11 +Assignment theme: **Systems & Reliability** — a workflow tool 
          +with an explicit                                              
       12 +state machine, guards on every transition, deterministic pure 
          +logic, an audit                                               
       13 +trail, and no server to run.                                  
       14  
       12 -- **Prototype, not a product.** Smallest thing that shows the 
          -workflow. Expand later.                                       
       13 -- **Zero-to-low setup.** Python + `pip install` + one command;
          - deployable to a free                                         
       14 -  host. No database, no auth, no backend service.             
       15 -- **Stateful workflow.** Each onboarding is a record with an e
          -xplicit lifecycle,                                            
       16 -  persisted, resumable, with a transition history (audit trail
          -).                                                            
       17 -- **One public catalog.** Private customers get a discount *po
          -licy* resolved against                                        
       18 -  the shared price book at billing time — we never fork the ca
          -talog per customer.                                           
       15 +## 2. Users                                                   
       16  
       20 -## Workflow                                                   
       17 +| User | Uses the tool to |                                   
       18 +|---|---|                                                     
       19 +| Sales | Author the agreement, send it for signature, provisi
          +on billing, generate a billing preview for a customer conversa
          +tion. |                                                       
       20 +| Billing team (consumer, not a user of the UI) | Receive the 
          +billing configuration JSON produced at provisioning. |        
       21  
       22 -```                                                           
       23 -DRAFT  ──generate_agreement──▶  AGREEMENT_READY  ──send_for_si
          -gnature──▶  PENDING_SIGNATURE  ──mark_signed──▶  SIGNED  ──pro
          -vision_billing──▶  ACTIVE                                     
       24 -  ▲                                    │                      
       25 -  └──────── update_agreement ──────────┘   (editing a generate
          -d agreement reverts it to DRAFT)                              
       26 -```                                                           
       22 +## 3. Vocabulary                                              
       23  
       28 -Guards:                                                       
       29 -- `generate_agreement` — the agreement must pass `validate_agr
          -eement` (required                                             
       30 -  fields, %s in 0–100, ≥1 discount, commitment > 0, known/uniq
          -ue services).                                                 
       31 -- `send_for_signature` — a document must have been generated. 
       32 -- `update_agreement` — only allowed in `DRAFT` / `AGREEMENT_RE
          -ADY`; locked once sent.                                       
       24 +| Term | Meaning |                                            
       25 +|---|---|                                                     
       26 +| Private pricing agreement | A committed-spend contract: an a
          +nnual dollar commitment plus a discount structure. |          
       27 +| Cross-service discount | A single percentage off list price 
          +that applies to every SKU. |                                  
       28 +| Service-specific discount (SSD) | A percentage off list pric
          +e for the SKUs of one service. |                              
       29 +| Service code | The stable machine identifier for a service (
          +`CLAUDE_API`, `CLAUDE_CODE`, `CLAUDE_FOR_WORK`, `SERVER_TOOLS`
          +). SSD rules match on this. |                                 
       30 +| Catalog | The public product catalog: SKUs with list prices.
          + One catalog for all customers. |                             
       31 +| Billing configuration | The JSON compiled at provisioning. H
          +olds the cross-service percentage and the SSD rules. Does not 
          +hold per-SKU prices. |                                        
       32 +| Rate table | Per-SKU discount and effective price. Derived f
          +rom the billing configuration and the current catalog on deman
          +d. Never stored. |                                            
       33 +| Billing preview | A non-binding cost estimate for one month,
          + computed from hand-entered usage. Not an invoice. |          
       34  
       34 -`apply(record, action, payload)` is the single mutation path —
          - copies the record,                                           
       35 -checks the transition, runs the guard, appends to `history`, r
          -eturns the new record.                                        
       36 -Pure; no I/O.                                                 
       35 +## 4. Scope                                                   
       36  
       38 -## Step 1 — Author agreement (fields)                         
       37 +### 4.1 In scope                                              
       38  
       40 -| Field | Notes |                                             
       39 +1. An onboarding tracker listing every onboarding with its sta
          +tus.                                                          
       40 +2. Agreement authoring: a form with the fields in section 6.  
       41 +3. Agreement generation: a one-page PDF from the form.        
       42 +4. A mock signature action that records a signer name and time
          +stamp.                                                        
       43 +5. Billing configuration: compile the agreement's discounts in
          +to the JSON in section 8.                                     
       44 +6. Rate table: resolve the billing configuration against the c
          +atalog.                                                       
       45 +7. Billing preview: one per month, from hand-entered usage, wi
          +th a PDF.                                                     
       46 +8. A state machine that governs every transition, with guards.
       47 +9. Persistence to one JSON file, surviving process restart.   
       48 +10. A per-record history log of every transition.             
       49 +11. Exports: billing configuration JSON, rate table CSV, billi
          +ng preview PDF, agreement PDF.                                
       50 +                                                              
       51 +### 4.2 Out of scope                                          
       52 +                                                              
       53 +1. A live usage-metering feed. Usage is typed in.             
       54 +2. Real electronic signature. The signature step is a button. 
       55 +3. Commitment drawdown, minimum-commitment shortfall billing, 
          +true-up, overage.                                             
       56 +4. Volume tiers, ramp schedules.                              
       57 +5. Per-SKU discount overrides in a contract exhibit.          
       58 +6. Flat (absolute) negotiated unit prices. Discounts are perce
          +ntages only.                                                  
       59 +7. Currencies other than USD.                                 
       60 +8. Multiple users, authentication, roles.                     
       61 +9. Tax.                                                       
       62 +10. Editing the catalog or price book through the UI.         
       63 +                                                              
       64 +## 5. State machine                                           
       65 +                                                              
       66 +Statuses: `DRAFT`, `AGREEMENT_READY`, `PENDING_SIGNATURE`, `SI
          +GNED`, `ACTIVE`.                                              
       67 +                                                              
       68 +| Action | From | To | Guard |                                
       69 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
       70 +| `generate_agreement` | `DRAFT` | `AGREEMENT_READY` | Agreeme
          +nt passes validation (section 7). |                           
       71 +| `send_for_signature` | `AGREEMENT_READY` | `PENDING_SIGNATUR
          +E` | A document was generated. |                              
       72 +| `mark_signed` | `PENDING_SIGNATURE` | `SIGNED` | — |        
       73 +| `provision_billing` | `SIGNED` | `ACTIVE` | Account number i
          +s present. |                                                  
       74 +| `update_agreement` | `DRAFT`, `AGREEMENT_READY` | `DRAFT` | 
          +Not allowed once sent for signature. From `AGREEMENT_READY` it
          + discards the generated document. |                           
       75 +| `record_billing_preview` | `ACTIVE` | `ACTIVE` | Billing mon
          +th is `YYYY-MM`; usage has at least one positive quantity. |  
       76 +                                                              
       77 +`apply(record, action, payload)` is the only function that cha
          +nges a record. It                                             
       78 +deep-copies the record, checks the transition is legal, runs t
          +he guard, applies                                             
       79 +the change, appends a `history` entry, and returns the new rec
          +ord. It performs no                                           
       80 +file or network I/O.                                          
       81 +                                                              
       82 +Every transition appends `{ at, action, from, to }` to `histor
          +y`. An                                                        
       83 +`update_agreement` from `AGREEMENT_READY` also appends `{ acti
          +on: "revise" }`. A                                            
       84 +`record_billing_preview` appends `note` set to the billing mon
          +th.                                                           
       85 +                                                              
       86 +## 6. Agreement fields                                        
       87 +                                                              
       88 +The Sales user enters:                                        
       89 +                                                              
       90 +| Field | Rule |                                              
       91  |---|---|
       42 -| Customer name | auto-filled `John Doe` / `Jane Doe`, editabl
          -e — the signer |                                              
       43 -| Company name | required |                                   
       44 -| Start date | default today |                                
       45 -| Term length | 12 / 24 / 36 months → end date derived (start 
          -+ term − 1 day) |                                             
       46 -| Annual committed spend (USD) | > 0 |                        
       47 -| Discount model | `cross_service` / `per_service` / `both` | 
       48 -| Cross-service % | 0–100, shown for cross-service or both |  
       49 -| Per-service rows | `service` (from catalog) + `%`, ≥1 for pe
          -r-service or both |                                           
       92 +| Customer name | Required. Pre-filled with `John Doe` or `Jan
          +e Doe` at record creation. This is the signer. |              
       93 +| Company name | Required. |                                  
       94 +| Account number | Required. The billing account the discount 
          +applies to. |                                                 
       95 +| Start date | Required. Defaults to today. |                 
       96 +| Term length | `12`, `24`, or `36` months. End date = start +
          + term − 1 day. |                                              
       97 +| Annual committed spend (USD) | Required. Greater than 0. |  
       98 +| Discount model | `cross_service`, `per_service`, or `both`. 
          +|                                                             
       99 +| Cross-service discount % | Shown and required when the model
          + is `cross_service` or `both`. 0 to 100. |                    
      100 +| Per-service rows | Shown when the model is `per_service` or 
          +`both`. Each row is a service (from the catalog) and a percent
          +age 0 to 100. At least one row. |                             
      101  
       51 -System-stamped, not shown: currency = USD, billing = monthly i
          -n arrears, account                                            
       52 -number (assigned at billing setup), id, status, timestamps, hi
          -story.                                                        
      102 +Set by the system, not shown as inputs: `currency = USD`, `bil
          +ling = monthly in                                             
      103 +arrears`, record id, status, timestamps, history.             
      104  
       54 -Explicitly cut for the prototype: contact email/address, sales
          - owner field, CRM id,                                         
       55 -auto-renew, payment terms, currency/billing-frequency choice, 
          -per-SKU exhibit                                               
       56 -overrides, flat-rate (absolute) pricing, a live coverage helpe

      105 +## 7. Agreement validation                                    
      106  
       58 -## Deliverables in this iteration                             
      107 +`generate_agreement` fails, and lists every problem, if any of
          + these hold:                                                  
      108  
       60 -Tracker · Step 1 form · generate agreement (+ PDF) · send for 
          -signature · mock                                              
       61 -"mark signed" · history log · JSON persistence · state-machine

      109 +1. Customer name is empty.

       63 -## Billing setup (`SIGNED → ACTIVE`) — built                  
      121 +## 8. Billing configuration

       75 -    { "id": "ssd-claude-code", "type": "service_specific_disco
          -unt",                                                         
       76 -      "description": "...", "discount_pct": 20,               
       77 -      "match": { "all": [ { "field": "service_code", "op": "eq
          -", "value": "CLAUDE_CODE" } ] } }                             
      127 +  "kind": "private-pricing-billing-config",                   
      128 +  "version": "1.0",                                           
      129 +  "onboarding_ref": "ONB-XXXXXXXX",                           
      130 +  "account_number": "GLBX-004417",                            
      131 +  "customer": { "company_name": "...", "signer_name": "..." },
      132 +  "currency": "USD",                                          
      133 +  "billing": "monthly in arrears",                            
      134 +  "effective_from": "2026-09-05",                             
      135 +  "effective_to": "2027-09-04",                               
      136 +  "annual_committed_spend_usd": 5000000.0,                    
      137 +  "price_book_date": "2026-08-01",                            
      138 +  "provisioned_at": "2026-09-07T17:42:41+00:00",              
      139 +  "cross_service_discount_pct": 12,          // integer, or nu
          +ll when the model has no cross-service part                   
      140 +  "discount_rules": [                        // one per per-se
          +rvice row; empty when the model is cross_service              
      141 +    {                                                         
      142 +      "id": "ssd-claude-code",                                
      143 +      "type": "service_specific_discount",                    
      144 +      "description": "Claude Code service discount",          
      145 +      "discount_pct": 20,                                     
      146 +      "match": { "all": [ { "field": "service_code", "op": "eq
          +", "value": "CLAUDE_CODE" } ] }                               
      147 +    }                                                         
      148    ]
      149  }
      150  ```
      151  
       82 -- **`match` grammar:** leaf `{field, op: eq|in, value}` over c
          -atalog fields                                                 
       83 -  (`service_code`, `id`, `unit`, `price_source`); combinators 
          -`{all:[...]}` / `{any:[...]}`.                                
       84 -- **Resolution (per SKU):** first matching `discount_rules` en
          -try → else                                                    
       85 -  `cross_service_discount_pct` if set → else list price. Prece
          -dence is by rule type                                         
       86 -  (service-specific beats cross-service); no explicit priority
          - field.                                                       
       87 -- The per-SKU **rate table is derived on demand** from config 
          -+ current catalog —                                           
       88 -  never persisted. Exports: config JSON, rate table CSV.      
       89 -- The account number is entered by Sales on the Step 1 form.  
      152 +### 8.1 Match expression grammar                              
      153  
       91 -## Billing preview (within ACTIVE) — built                    
      154 +- Leaf: `{ "field": F, "op": OP, "value": V }`. `F` is one of 
          +`service_code`, `id`,                                         
      155 +  `unit`, `price_source`. `OP` is `eq` or `in`. For `in`, `V` 
          +is a list.                                                    
      156 +- Combinator: `{ "all": [ expr, ... ] }` (AND) or `{ "any": [ 
          +expr, ... ] }` (OR).                                          
      157 +- An unknown field or op is an error.                         
      158  
       93 -A **non-binding estimate**, not an invoice: usage is hand-ente
          -red and Sales, not                                            
       94 -finance, runs it. `record_billing_preview` (a guarded mutation
          -, not a state                                                 
       95 -transition — status stays `ACTIVE`) takes a billing month + pe
          -r-SKU usage and                                               
       96 -appends a `private-pricing-billing-preview` to `record["billin
          -g_previews"]` (one                                            
       97 -per month; regenerating a month replaces it; logged in history
          -).                                                            
      159 +### 8.2 Resolution                                            
      160  
       99 -- `build_billing_preview(config, month, usage, catalog)` — for
          - each SKU with                                                
      100 -  usage > 0: resolve its rate; `gross_amount = qty × list_pric
          -e`,                                                           
      101 -  `amount = qty × net_unit_price`. Carries `gross_subtotal` (a
          -t list),                                                      
      102 -  `discount_total` + `savings_pct` (what the agreement saves),
          - `subtotal`/`total`                                           
      103 -  (at negotiated rates).                                      
      104 -- **Simple metered math** — no commitment drawdown, true-up, o
          -r overage.                                                    
      105 -- UX: editable full-catalog usage table (net unit price read-o
          -nly) + "Load sample                                           
      106 -  usage"; a live "list vs your rate vs saving" total updates a
          -s quantities change.                                          
      107 -- PDF titled "Billing Preview" with a "not an invoice" disclai
          -mer; per-line                                                 
      108 -  list/discount/net and list-vs-negotiated totals.            
      161 +For each SKU in the catalog:                                  
      162  
      110 -Later: per-SKU override rules; usage/volume tiers evaluated at
          - rating time;                                                 
      111 -commitment drawdown + true-up; real e-signature; multi-user.  
      163 +1. Evaluate `discount_rules` in order. The first rule whose `m
          +atch` is satisfied                                            
      164 +   applies its `discount_pct`; record its `id` as the applied 
          +rule.                                                         
      165 +2. If no rule matched and `cross_service_discount_pct` is set,
          + apply it; record the                                         
      166 +   applied rule as `cross_service`.                           
      167 +3. Otherwise the SKU bills at list price; the applied rule is 
          +null.                                                         
      168 +                                                              
      169 +`effective_price = round(list_price × (1 − pct/100), 4)` for `
          +per_mtok` SKUs,                                               
      170 +2 decimals otherwise.                                         
      171 +                                                              
      172 +The rate table is `{ lines: [...], summary: { skus_total, skus
          +_discounted,                                                  
      173 +skus_at_list, skus_at_list_ids } }`. It is computed on request
          +. It is not written                                           
      174 +to the record or the store.                                   
      175 +                                                              
      176 +## 9. Billing preview                                         
      177 +                                                              
      178 +`record_billing_preview` takes a billing month (`YYYY-MM`) and
          + a map of SKU id to                                           
      179 +quantity. It appends this JSON to `record.billing_previews`:  
      180 +                                                              
      181 +```jsonc                                                      
      182 +{                                                             
      183 +  "kind": "private-pricing-billing-preview",

      186 +  "account_number": "GLBX-004417",

      188 +  "config_ref": "ONB-XXXXXXXX",                               
      189 +  "billing_period": "2026-09",                                
      190 +  "generated_at": "2026-09-07T17:42:41+00:00",                
      191 +  "currency": "USD",

      193 +  "lines": [                                                  
      194 +    {                                                         
      195 +      "sku_id": "API-OPUS-5-INPUT",                           
      196 +      "description": "Claude Opus 5 - input tokens",          
      197 +      "service_code": "CLAUDE_API",                           
      198 +      "unit": "per_mtok",                                     
      199 +      "quantity": 120.0,                                      
      200 +      "list_price": 5.0,                                      
      201 +      "discount_pct": 10,                                     
      202 +      "unit_price": 4.5,                                      
      203 +      "applied_rule": "cross_service",                        
      204 +      "gross_amount": 600.0,                                  
      205 +      "amount": 540.0                                         
      206 +    }                                                         
      207 +  ],                                                          
      208 +  "gross_subtotal": 5850.0,     // sum of gross_amount, at lis
          +t price                                                       
      209 +  "discount_total": 783.0,      // gross_subtotal − subtotal  
      210 +  "savings_pct": 13.4,          // discount_total / gross_subt
          +otal × 100, 1 decimal                                         
      211 +  "subtotal": 5067.0,           // sum of amount, at negotiate
          +d rates                                                       
      212 +  "total": 5067.0                                             
      213 +}                                                             
      214 +```                                                           
      215 +                                                              
      216 +Rules:                                                        
      217 +                                                              
      218 +1. Only SKUs with quantity greater than 0 produce a line. Unkn
          +own SKU ids are                                               
      219 +   dropped.                                                   
      220 +2. `gross_amount = round(quantity × list_price, 2)`. `amount =
          + round(quantity ×                                             
      221 +   effective_price, 2)`.                                      
      222 +3. One preview per billing month. Generating a preview for a m
          +onth that already                                             
      223 +   has one replaces it.                                       
      224 +4. The preview is a cost estimate. It applies no commitment dr
          +awdown, true-up, or                                           
      225 +   overage.

      288 +                                                              
      289 +## 13. Non-functional requirements                            
      290 +                                                              
      291 +1. **Stack**: Python 3.11 or later, Streamlit, `fpdf2`. No oth
          +er runtime                                                    
      292 +   dependency.                                                
      293 +2. **No backend**: no server process of its own, no database, 
          +no authentication.                                            
      294 +3. **Persistence**: one JSON file, `data/onboardings.json`, ho
          +lding a map of                                                
      295 +   record id to record. Writes are atomic (write a temp file, 
          +rename over the                                               
      296 +   target). The file is not committed.                        
      297 +4. **Purity**: `workflow.py` and `engine.py` import no Streaml
          +it and perform no                                             
      298 +   file or network I/O. They are covered by `unittest` with no
          + third-party test                                             
      299 +   dependency.                                                
      300 +5. **Determinism**: amounts round to 2 decimals; `per_mtok` ef
          +fective unit prices                                           
      301 +   round to 4. The screen, the JSON, and the CSV show the same
          + numbers.                                                     
      302 +6. **Timestamps**: ISO 8601, UTC, second precision.           
      303 +7. **Run**: `pip install -r requirements.txt` then `streamlit 
          +run app.py`.                                                  
      304 +8. **Deploy**: the repository runs on Streamlit Community Clou
          +d with no change.                                             
      305 +

  ⎿  Added 18 lines, removed 2 lines
       1  # Requirements — Private Pricing Customer Onboarding
       2  
       3 -## 1. Overview                                                 
       3 +## 1. Problem                                                  
       4  
       5 +Large customers sign committed-spend contracts: a dollar commit
         +ment per year in                                               
       6 +exchange for negotiated discounts. Getting one of those custome
         +rs set up to bill                                              
       7 +correctly is a hand-off across several steps — agree terms, dra
         +ft an agreement,                                               
       8 +get it signed, translate the negotiated discounts into per-SKU 
         +rates the billing                                              
       9 +system applies — and each step is done ad hoc in spreadsheets a
         +nd email. Steps get                                            
      10 +skipped. Discounts get entered against the wrong products. The 
         +discount definition                                            
      11 +drifts from the products it covers. No single record shows wher
         +e a given customer                                             
      12 +is in the process.                                             
      13 +                                                               
      14 +This tool is one workflow that carries a customer from a draft 
         +agreement to active                                            
      15 +billing. Every step is a guarded state transition with an audit
         + trail. The                                                    
      16 +negotiated discounts are stored as rules that resolve against o
         +ne shared product                                              
      17 +catalog, not as a per-customer price list that has to be mainta
         +ined by hand.                                                  
      18 +                                                               
      19 +## 2. Overview                                                 
      20 +                                                               
      21  A web tool for a Sales user to onboard a customer onto a privat
          e (committed-spend)
       6 -pricing agreement, and to run that agreement afterward. The use
         -r authors the deal                                             
      22 +pricing agreement and to run that agreement afterward. The user
         + authors the deal                                              
      23  terms, the tool produces a signable agreement PDF, the customer
           signs (mocked),
      24  the tool compiles a billing configuration, and the user generat
          es a monthly
      25  billing preview from usage.

⏺ Done, committed (ffec720). The doc is in the project repo and now opens with
  the problem statement.

  Path: /Users/vksatish/private-pricing-customer-onboarding/docs/REQUIREMENTS.md

  Section 1 — Problem now leads:

  ▎ Large customers sign committed-spend contracts: a dollar commitment per year
  ▎ in exchange for negotiated discounts. Getting one of those customers set up
  ▎ to bill correctly is a hand-off across several steps — agree terms, draft 
  ▎ an agreement, get it signed, translate the negotiated discounts into per-SKU
  ▎ rates the billing system applies — and each step is done ad hoc in 
  ▎ spreadsheets and email. Steps get skipped. Discounts get entered against the
  ▎ wrong products. The discount definition drifts from the products it covers.
  ▎ No single record shows where a given customer is in the process.
  ▎
  ▎ This tool is one workflow that carries a customer from a draft agreement to 
  ▎ active billing. Every step is a guarded state transition with an audit 
  ▎ trail. The negotiated discounts are stored as rules that resolve against one
  ▎ shared product catalog, not as a per-customer price list that has to be 
  ▎ maintained by hand.

  Sections renumbered 1–16, all four "section N" cross-references corrected (→
  7, 9, 8, 7). It's committed as part of the repo, so it'll go up whenever you
  push.

⏺ Added and committed (b9f7a72). New section 6 — Assumptions:

  ▎ 1. A metering application exists and measures each customer's usage per SKU 
  ▎ per billing period. This tool does not meter usage. The billing 
  ▎ configuration it produces (section 10) is the input a downstream billing 
  ▎ process uses, together with metered usage, to compute the customer's bill: 
  ▎ for each SKU, charge = metered_quantity × list_price × (1 − discount_pct / 
  ▎ 100), with discount_pct resolved from the configuration.
  ▎ 2. The billing preview in this tool stands in for that downstream 
  ▎ calculation using hand-entered quantities. It is an estimate, not the bill.
  ▎ 3. The public catalog and its list prices are maintained elsewhere and are 
  ▎ current. This tool reads them.
  ▎ 4. Agreement terms are negotiated and internally approved before a user 
  ▎ enters them here. This tool records terms; it does not run deal approval.
  ▎ 5. One account number identifies one customer billing account. A 
  ▎ configuration's discounts apply to that account.

  Sections renumbered 1–17; the four "section N" cross-references updated (→ 8,
  9, 10).

❯ remove this two ## 8. Agreement fields                                        
                                                                                
  The Sales user enters:                                                        
                                                                                
  | Field | Rule |                                                              
  |---|---|                                                                     
  | Customer name | Required. Pre-filled with John Doe or Jane Doe at record    
  creation. This is the signer. |                                               
  | Company name | Required. |                                                  
  | Account number | Required. The billing account the discount applies to. |   
  | Start date | Required. Defaults to today. |                                 
  | Term length | 12, 24, or 36 months. End date = start + term − 1 day. |      
  | Annual committed spend (USD) | Required. Greater than 0. |                  
  | Discount model | cross_service, per_service, or both. |                     
  | Cross-service discount % | Shown and required when the model is             
  cross_service or both. 0 to 100. |                                            
  | Per-service rows | Shown when the model is per_service or both. Each row is 
  a service (from the catalog) and a percentage 0 to 100. At least one row. |   
                                                                                
  Set by the system, not shown as inputs: currency = USD, billing = monthly in  
  arrears, record id, status, timestamps, history.                              
                                                                                
  9. Agreement validation                                                       
                                                                                
  generate_agreement fails, and lists every problem, if any of these hold:      
                                                                                
  1. Customer name is empty.                                                    
  2. Company name is empty.                                                     
  3. Account number is empty.                                                   
  4. Annual committed spend is not greater than 0.                              
  5. Term length is not 12, 24, or 36.                                          
  6. Discount model is not one of the three values.                             
  7. Model needs a cross-service percentage and it is missing or outside 0–100. 
  8. Model needs per-service rows and there are none.                           
  9. A per-service row names a service that is not in the catalog.              
  10. A service appears in more than one per-service row.                       
  11. A per-service percentage is missing or outside 0–100.

※ recap: You're building a Streamlit take-home prototype: a Sales workflow to onboard a customer onto private committed-spend pricing and provision billing. ❯ why do we have this requirements.txt ?                                        

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ It's the Python dependency manifest — the list of third-party packages the app
  needs to run:

  streamlit>=1.40
  fpdf2>=2.8

  It exists so the app is reproducible without you telling anyone what to
  install:

  - A reviewer runs pip install -r requirements.txt and gets exactly what's
    needed — nothing more (the standard library covers json, unittest, csv,
    datetime, etc., so those aren't listed).
  - Streamlit Community Cloud reads this file automatically on deploy and builds
    the environment from it. Without it, the hosted app wouldn't start.
  - It's the convention Python tooling expects at a repo root, so pip, CI, and
    Docker all just work.

  Only two lines because the design keeps the dependency surface small on
  purpose — workflow.py and engine.py use zero third-party packages, so the
  tests need nothing installed at all.

  /export
